Then we deploy the StatefulSet: Patroni config, RBAC, the rw and ro services, and spread constraints.


hmsmiraz@HMSMIRAZ:~/postgres-ha-lab$ tee -a versions.env > /dev/null <<'EOF' POSTGRES_BASE_IMAGE=postgres:18.7-trixie@sha256:e754b839f447639b7004895f4d8a4bfcb4e9dbe3a1bc035f27f646562a16fd61 PGBACKREST_APT_VERSION=2.60.0-1.pgdg13+1 IMAGE_NAME=pg-ha IMAGE_TAG=18.7-patroni4.1.5-pgbackrest2.60.0 EO

PASTED
The image is built and committed (aec6da4). ALL CHECKS PASSED shows postgres 18.7, patroni 4.1.5 and pgBackRest 2.60.0, all running as uid 999, and the failing run before it is saved too.

The build printed 2 warnings, and they’re mine. The ARG BASE_IMAGE has no default, and it isn’t re-declared after FROM, so the label gets an empty value. The # syntax= line also pulls an unpinned Dockerfile frontend over the network. Both are fixed in step 1.

Phase 2, step 3: deploy Patroni on Kubernetes
This is the core of the assignment, so the pieces are separate files that we can review and test one at a time. Two design points:

Restricted Pod Security from day one. The namespace enforces the restricted profile, so the pods can’t run as root or with extra capabilities.
A name collision I avoided. Patroni creates its own ConfigMaps named <scope>-config, <scope>-sync and so on. Our static config is named patroni-static-config so the two never collide.
1. Fix the Dockerfile and rebuild

tee image/Dockerfile > /dev/null <<'EOF'
ARG BASE_IMAGE=postgres:18.7-trixie
FROM ${BASE_IMAGE}

ARG BASE_IMAGE
ARG PGBACKREST_APT_VERSION
ARG PATRONI_VERSION
ARG POSTGRES_VERSION

LABEL org.opencontainers.image.title="pg-ha" \
      org.opencontainers.image.description="PostgreSQL with Patroni and pgBackRest" \
      org.opencontainers.image.base.name="${BASE_IMAGE}"

# pgBackRest from the PGDG repo (already configured in the official image),
# tini as PID 1 so zombie processes are reaped, python for Patroni.
RUN apt-get update \
 && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
      python3 python3-venv tini \
      "pgbackrest=${PGBACKREST_APT_VERSION}" \
 && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

# Patroni and its dependencies from the committed lock file, in a venv.
COPY image/requirements.lock /tmp/requirements.lock
RUN python3 -m venv /opt/patroni \
 && /opt/patroni/bin/pip install --no-cache-dir --require-virtualenv -r /tmp/requirements.lock \
 && rm /tmp/requirements.lock

ENV PATH="/opt/patroni/bin:${PATH}"

RUN mkdir -p /pgdata /etc/patroni \
 && chown postgres:postgres /pgdata \
 && chmod 700 /pgdata

USER postgres
WORKDIR /home/postgres

# Patroni starts and stops Postgres itself, so we do not use the stock entrypoint.
ENTRYPOINT ["tini", "--", "patroni"]
CMD ["/etc/patroni/patroni.yml"]
EOF
bash scripts/image-build.sh 2>&1 | tail -8
bash tests/image-build.sh 2>&1 | tee docs/evidence/IMG-build.txt
The build should show no warnings, and the test should end with ALL CHECKS PASSED.

2. Create the manifests

mkdir -p k8s/pg
tee k8s/pg/00-namespace.yaml > /dev/null <<'EOF'
apiVersion: v1
kind: Namespace
metadata:
  name: pg-ha
  labels:
    pod-security.kubernetes.io/enforce: restricted
    pod-security.kubernetes.io/enforce-version: latest
EOF

tee k8s/pg/10-rbac.yaml > /dev/null <<'EOF'
apiVersion: v1
kind: ServiceAccount
metadata:
  name: patroni
  namespace: pg-ha
---
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: Role
metadata:
  name: patroni
  namespace: pg-ha
rules:
  # Leader lock, cluster config and sync state live in ConfigMaps.
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["configmaps"]
    verbs: ["create", "get", "list", "patch", "update", "watch", "delete", "deletecollection"]
  # Patroni sets the role label on its own pod.
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["pods"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "patch", "update", "watch"]
---
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: RoleBinding
metadata:
  name: patroni
  namespace: pg-ha
roleRef:
  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
  kind: Role
  name: patroni
subjects:
  - kind: ServiceAccount
    name: patroni
    namespace: pg-ha
EOF

tee k8s/pg/20-config.yaml > /dev/null <<'EOF'
apiVersion: v1
kind: ConfigMap
metadata:
  name: patroni-static-config
  namespace: pg-ha
data:
  patroni.yml: |
    scope: pg-ha
    restapi:
      listen: 0.0.0.0:8008
    kubernetes:
      use_endpoints: false
      scope_label: cluster-name
      role_label: role
      leader_label_value: primary
      standby_leader_label_value: primary
      follower_label_value: replica
      labels:
        application: pg-ha
    bootstrap:
      dcs:
        ttl: 30
        loop_wait: 10
        retry_timeout: 10
        maximum_lag_on_failover: 1048576
        synchronous_mode: true
        synchronous_mode_strict: false
        synchronous_node_count: 1
        postgresql:
          use_pg_rewind: true
          use_slots: true
          parameters:
            wal_level: replica
            hot_standby: "on"
            max_wal_senders: 10
            max_replication_slots: 10
            wal_log_hints: "on"
            max_slot_wal_keep_size: 1GB
            password_encryption: scram-sha-256
            unix_socket_directories: /run/postgresql
      initdb:
        - encoding: UTF8
        - data-checksums
      post_bootstrap: sh /etc/patroni/post-bootstrap.sh
    postgresql:
      listen: 0.0.0.0:5432
      data_dir: /pgdata/data
      bin_dir: /usr/lib/postgresql/18/bin
      use_unix_socket: true
      pgpass: /tmp/pgpass
      authentication:
        superuser:
          username: postgres
        replication:
          username: replicator
        rewind:
          username: rewind_user
      pg_hba:
        - local all postgres peer
        - host all all 127.0.0.1/32 scram-sha-256
        - host replication replicator 192.168.0.0/16 scram-sha-256
        - host all all 192.168.0.0/16 scram-sha-256
  post-bootstrap.sh: |
    #!/bin/sh
    # Runs once on the first primary. The app password comes from the environment.
    set -eu
    psql "$1" -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -v app_pw="$APP_PASSWORD" <<'SQL'
    CREATE ROLE app LOGIN PASSWORD :'app_pw';
    CREATE DATABASE appdb OWNER app;
    SQL
EOF

tee k8s/pg/30-services.yaml > /dev/null <<'EOF'
# Stable read-write endpoint: always the current primary.
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: pg-primary
  namespace: pg-ha
spec:
  selector:
    application: pg-ha
    cluster-name: pg-ha
    role: primary
  ports:
    - name: postgres
      port: 5432
      targetPort: 5432
---
# Read-only endpoint: replicas only.
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: pg-replica
  namespace: pg-ha
spec:
  selector:
    application: pg-ha
    cluster-name: pg-ha
    role: replica
  ports:
    - name: postgres
      port: 5432
      targetPort: 5432
---
# Stable per-pod DNS names for the StatefulSet.
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: pg-ha-headless
  namespace: pg-ha
spec:
  clusterIP: None
  selector:
    application: pg-ha
    cluster-name: pg-ha
  ports:
    - name: postgres
      port: 5432
    - name: patroni
      port: 8008
EOF

tee k8s/pg/40-pdb.yaml > /dev/null <<'EOF'
apiVersion: policy/v1
kind: PodDisruptionBudget
metadata:
  name: pg-ha
  namespace: pg-ha
spec:
  minAvailable: 2
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      application: pg-ha
      cluster-name: pg-ha
EOF
3. Create the StatefulSet. __IMAGE__ is replaced from versions.env by the deploy script.

tee k8s/pg/50-statefulset.yaml > /dev/null <<'EOF'
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: StatefulSet
metadata:
  name: pg-ha
  namespace: pg-ha
  labels:
    application: pg-ha
    cluster-name: pg-ha
spec:
  serviceName: pg-ha-headless
  replicas: 3
  podManagementPolicy: Parallel
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      application: pg-ha
      cluster-name: pg-ha
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        application: pg-ha
        cluster-name: pg-ha
    spec:
      serviceAccountName: patroni
      terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 60
      securityContext:
        runAsNonRoot: true
        runAsUser: 999
        runAsGroup: 999
        fsGroup: 999
        seccompProfile:
          type: RuntimeDefault
      affinity:
        podAntiAffinity:
          requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution:
            - topologyKey: kubernetes.io/hostname
              labelSelector:
                matchLabels:
                  application: pg-ha
                  cluster-name: pg-ha
      topologySpreadConstraints:
        - maxSkew: 1
          topologyKey: topology.kubernetes.io/zone
          whenUnsatisfiable: DoNotSchedule
          labelSelector:
            matchLabels:
              application: pg-ha
              cluster-name: pg-ha
      containers:
        - name: postgres
          image: __IMAGE__
          imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent
          ports:
            - name: postgres
              containerPort: 5432
            - name: patroni
              containerPort: 8008
          env:
            - name: PATRONI_NAME
              valueFrom:
                fieldRef:
                  fieldPath: metadata.name
            - name: PATRONI_KUBERNETES_NAMESPACE
              valueFrom:
                fieldRef:
                  fieldPath: metadata.namespace
            - name: POD_IP
              valueFrom:
                fieldRef:
                  fieldPath: status.podIP
            - name: PATRONI_KUBERNETES_POD_IP
              value: "$(POD_IP)"
            - name: PATRONI_RESTAPI_CONNECT_ADDRESS
              value: "$(POD_IP):8008"
            - name: PATRONI_POSTGRESQL_CONNECT_ADDRESS
              value: "$(POD_IP):5432"
            - name: PATRONI_SUPERUSER_PASSWORD
              valueFrom:
                secretKeyRef:
                  name: pg-credentials
                  key: superuser-password
            - name: PATRONI_REPLICATION_PASSWORD
              valueFrom:
                secretKeyRef:
                  name: pg-credentials
                  key: replication-password
            - name: PATRONI_REWIND_PASSWORD
              valueFrom:
                secretKeyRef:
                  name: pg-credentials
                  key: rewind-password
            - name: PATRONI_RESTAPI_USERNAME
              value: patroni
            - name: PATRONI_RESTAPI_PASSWORD
              valueFrom:
                secretKeyRef:
                  name: pg-credentials
                  key: restapi-password
            - name: APP_PASSWORD
              valueFrom:
                secretKeyRef:
                  name: pg-credentials
                  key: app-password
          startupProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /liveness
              port: 8008
            periodSeconds: 5
            failureThreshold: 60
          livenessProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /liveness
              port: 8008
            periodSeconds: 10
            failureThreshold: 6
          readinessProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /readiness
              port: 8008
            periodSeconds: 5
            failureThreshold: 3
          resources:
            requests:
              cpu: 100m
              memory: 256Mi
            limits:
              memory: 768Mi
          securityContext:
            allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
            capabilities:
              drop: ["ALL"]
          volumeMounts:
            - name: data
              mountPath: /pgdata
            - name: patroni-config
              mountPath: /etc/patroni
              readOnly: true
            - name: run
              mountPath: /run/postgresql
            - name: tmp
              mountPath: /tmp
            - name: home
              mountPath: /home/postgres
      volumes:
        - name: patroni-config
          configMap:
            name: patroni-static-config
        - name: run
          emptyDir: {}
        - name: tmp
          emptyDir: {}
        - name: home
          emptyDir: {}
  volumeClaimTemplates:
    - metadata:
        name: data
      spec:
        accessModes: ["ReadWriteOnce"]
        resources:
          requests:
            storage: 2Gi
EOF
4. Create the scripts. Secrets are generated with openssl and passed through process substitution, so no password appears in a process list or in the repo.

tee scripts/gen-secrets.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Generate database credentials once. Never committed, never printed.
set -euo pipefail
NS=pg-ha

if kubectl -n "$NS" get secret pg-credentials >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "secret pg-credentials already exists, keeping it"
  exit 0
fi

gen() { printf '%s' "$(openssl rand -base64 33 | tr -d '/+=' | cut -c1-32)"; }

kubectl -n "$NS" create secret generic pg-credentials \
  --from-file=superuser-password=<(gen) \
  --from-file=replication-password=<(gen) \
  --from-file=rewind-password=<(gen) \
  --from-file=restapi-password=<(gen) \
  --from-file=app-password=<(gen)
EOF

tee scripts/pg-up.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Deploy the Patroni PostgreSQL cluster into the kind cluster.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source versions.env

IMG="${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}"
kind load docker-image "$IMG" --name kind

kubectl apply -f k8s/pg/00-namespace.yaml
bash scripts/gen-secrets.sh
kubectl apply -f k8s/pg/10-rbac.yaml -f k8s/pg/20-config.yaml \
              -f k8s/pg/30-services.yaml -f k8s/pg/40-pdb.yaml
sed "s|__IMAGE__|${IMG}|g" k8s/pg/50-statefulset.yaml | kubectl apply -f -
kubectl -n pg-ha rollout status sts/pg-ha --timeout=600s
EOF
chmod +x scripts/gen-secrets.sh scripts/pg-up.sh
5. Write the tests first. rep-topology.sh covers REP-1 and con-endpoints.sh covers CON-1 and CON-2.

tee tests/rep-topology.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# REP-1: 1 primary + 2 streaming replicas, PostgreSQL >= 15.
set -euo pipefail
NS=pg-ha
pass() { echo "PASS: $*"; }
fail() { echo "FAIL: $*"; exit 1; }
ctl() { kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml "$@"; }

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
leaders=0; followers=0
deadline=$((SECONDS + 300))
while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
  state=$(ctl list -f json 2>/dev/null || true)
  leaders=$(jq '[.[] | select(.Role == "Leader")] | length' <<<"$state" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  followers=$(jq '[.[] | select((.Role == "Replica" or .Role == "Sync Standby") and .State == "streaming")] | length' <<<"$state" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  if [ "$leaders" = 1 ] && [ "$followers" = 2 ]; then break; fi
  sleep 5
done

ctl list || true
[ "$leaders" = 1 ] || fail "expected 1 leader, found $leaders"
pass "1 leader"
[ "$followers" = 2 ] || fail "expected 2 streaming replicas, found $followers"
pass "2 streaming replicas"

ver=$(kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atc "show server_version_num")
[ "$ver" -ge 150000 ] || fail "PostgreSQL version $ver is older than 15"
pass "PostgreSQL version number $ver (>= 15)"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF

tee tests/con-endpoints.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# CON-1: pg-primary always reaches the primary. CON-2: pg-replica reaches replicas only.
set -euo pipefail
NS=pg-ha
pass() { echo "PASS: $*"; }
fail() { echo "FAIL: $*"; exit 1; }

# Run SQL as the app user against a service, from inside pg-ha-0.
q() {
  kubectl -n "$NS" exec -i pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- sh -c \
    'PGPASSWORD="$APP_PASSWORD" psql -h "$0" -U app -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1' "$1" <<<"$2"
}

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
rw=$(q pg-primary "select pg_is_in_recovery()") || fail "cannot connect through pg-primary"
[ "$rw" = "f" ] || fail "pg-primary is in recovery (not a primary): $rw"
pass "pg-primary reaches a primary (pg_is_in_recovery = f)"

q pg-primary "create table if not exists con_probe(id serial primary key, ts timestamptz default now()); insert into con_probe default values;" >/dev/null \
  || fail "write through pg-primary failed"
pass "write through pg-primary works"

ro=$(q pg-replica "select pg_is_in_recovery()") || fail "cannot connect through pg-replica"
[ "$ro" = "t" ] || fail "pg-replica is not a replica: $ro"
pass "pg-replica reaches a replica (pg_is_in_recovery = t)"

if q pg-replica "create table should_fail(i int)" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  fail "write through pg-replica was accepted"
fi
pass "write through pg-replica is rejected"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/rep-topology.sh tests/con-endpoints.sh
shellcheck scripts/gen-secrets.sh scripts/pg-up.sh tests/rep-topology.sh tests/con-endpoints.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"

If this fails because there’s no schema for 1.37.0, retry with -kubernetes-version 1.34.0 and tell me. Don’t skip it.

If a pod doesn’t become ready
First deployments usually need one or two fixes, and that’s normal. Don’t delete anything. Send me:

kubectl -n pg-ha get pods -o wide
kubectl -n pg-ha logs pg-ha-0 -c postgres --tail=40
kubectl -n pg-ha get events --sort-by=.lastTimestamp | tail -15
A Forbidden error would point at the RBAC rules, and a FailedCreate event at the restricted Pod Security profile.

apiVersion: v1
kind: ConfigMap
metadata:
  name: patroni-static-config
  namespace: pg-ha
data:
  patroni.yml: |
    scope: pg-ha
    restapi:
      listen: 0.0.0.0:8008

    bootstrap:
      dcs:
        ttl: 30
        loop_wait: 10
        retry_timeout: 10
        maximum_lag_on_failover: 1048576
        synchronous_mode: true
        synchronous_mode_strict: false
        synchronous_node_count: 1
        postgresql:
          use_pg_rewind: true
          use_slots: true
          parameters:
            wal_level: replica
            hot_standby: "on"
            max_wal_senders: 10
            max_replication_slots: 10
            wal_log_hints: "on"
            max_slot_wal_keep_size: 1GB
            password_encryption: scram-sha-256
            unix_socket_directories: /run/postgresql
      initdb:
        - encoding: UTF8
        - data-checksums
      post_bootstrap: sh /etc/patroni/post-bootstrap.sh
    postgresql:
      listen: 0.0.0.0:5432
      data_dir: /pgdata/data
      bin_dir: /usr/lib/postgresql/18/bin
      use_unix_socket: true
      pgpass: /tmp/pgpass
      authentication:
        superuser:
          username: postgres
        replication:
          username: replicator
        rewind:
          username: rewind_user
      pg_hba:
        - local all postgres peer
        - host all all 127.0.0.1/32 scram-sha-256
        - host replication replicator 192.168.0.0/16 scram-sha-256
        - host all all 192.168.0.0/16 scram-sha-256
  post-bootstrap.sh: |
    #!/bin/sh
    # Runs once on the first primary. The app password comes from the environment.
    set -eu
    psql "$1" -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -v app_pw="$APP_PASSWORD" <<'SQL'
    CREATE ROLE app LOGIN PASSWORD :'app_pw';
    CREATE DATABASE appdb OWNER app;
    SQL
EOF

tee k8s/pg/30-services.yaml > /dev/null <<'EOF'
# Stable read-write endpoint: always the current primary.
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: pg-primary
  namespace: pg-ha
spec:
  selector:
    application: pg-ha
    cluster-name: pg-ha
    role: primary
  ports:
    - name: postgres
      port: 5432
      targetPort: 5432
---
# Read-only endpoint: replicas only.
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: pg-replica
  namespace: pg-ha
spec:
  selector:
    application: pg-ha
    cluster-name: pg-ha
    role: replica
  ports:
    - name: postgres
      port: 5432
      targetPort: 5432
---
# Stable per-pod DNS names for the StatefulSet.
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: pg-ha-headless
  namespace: pg-ha
spec:
  clusterIP: None
  selector:
    application: pg-ha
    cluster-name: pg-ha
  ports:
    - name: postgres
      port: 5432
    - name: patroni
      port: 8008
EOF

5. Create the deploy scripts
Passwords are generated with openssl and sent over stdin, so they never appear in a process list or in the repo.

tee scripts/gen-secrets.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Generate database credentials once. Never committed, never printed.
set -euo pipefail
NS=pg-ha

if kubectl -n "$NS" get secret pg-credentials >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "secret pg-credentials already exists, keeping it"
  exit 0
fi

gen() { openssl rand -base64 33 | tr -d '/+=' | cut -c1-32; }

kubectl -n "$NS" apply -f - <<YAML
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: pg-credentials
type: Opaque
stringData:
  superuser-password: "$(gen)"
  replication-password: "$(gen)"
  rewind-password: "$(gen)"
  restapi-password: "$(gen)"
  app-password: "$(gen)"
YAML
EOF

tee scripts/pg-up.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Deploy the Patroni PostgreSQL cluster into the kind cluster.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source versions.env

IMG="${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}"
kind load docker-image "$IMG" --name kind

kubectl apply -f k8s/pg/00-namespace.yaml
bash scripts/gen-secrets.sh
kubectl apply -f k8s/pg/10-rbac.yaml -f k8s/pg/20-config.yaml \
              -f k8s/pg/30-services.yaml -f k8s/pg/40-pdb.yaml
sed "s|__IMAGE__|${IMG}|g" k8s/pg/50-statefulset.yaml | kubectl apply -f -
kubectl -n pg-ha rollout status sts/pg-ha --timeout=600s
EOF
chmod +x scripts/gen-secrets.sh scripts/pg-up.sh
6. Write the acceptance tests first
rep-topology.sh covers REP-1, and con-endpoints.sh covers CON-1 and CON-2.

tee tests/rep-topology.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# REP-1: 1 primary + 2 streaming replicas, PostgreSQL >= 15.
set -euo pipefail
NS=pg-ha
pass() { echo "PASS: $*"; }
fail() { echo "FAIL: $*"; exit 1; }
ctl() { kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml "$@"; }

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
leaders=0; followers=0
deadline=$((SECONDS + 300))
while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
  state=$(ctl list -f json 2>/dev/null || true)
  leaders=$(jq '[.[] | select(.Role == "Leader")] | length' <<<"$state" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  followers=$(jq '[.[] | select((.Role == "Replica" or .Role == "Sync Standby") and .State == "streaming")] | length' <<<"$state" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  if [ "$leaders" = 1 ] && [ "$followers" = 2 ]; then break; fi
  sleep 5
done

ctl list || true
[ "$leaders" = 1 ] || fail "expected 1 leader, found $leaders"
pass "1 leader"
[ "$followers" = 2 ] || fail "expected 2 streaming replicas, found $followers"
pass "2 streaming replicas"

ver=$(kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atc "show server_version_num")
[ "$ver" -ge 150000 ] || fail "PostgreSQL version $ver is older than 15"
pass "PostgreSQL version number $ver (>= 15)"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF

tee tests/con-endpoints.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# CON-1: pg-primary always reaches the primary. CON-2: pg-replica reaches replicas only.
set -euo pipefail
NS=pg-ha
pass() { echo "PASS: $*"; }
fail() { echo "FAIL: $*"; exit 1; }

# Run SQL as the app user against a service, from inside pg-ha-0.
q() {
  kubectl -n "$NS" exec -i pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- sh -c \
    'PGPASSWORD="$APP_PASSWORD" psql -h "$0" -U app -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1' "$1" <<<"$2"
}

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
rw=$(q pg-primary "select pg_is_in_recovery()") || fail "cannot connect through pg-primary"
[ "$rw" = "f" ] || fail "pg-primary is in recovery (not a primary): $rw"
pass "pg-primary reaches a primary (pg_is_in_recovery = f)"

q pg-primary "create table if not exists con_probe(id serial primary key, ts timestamptz default now()); insert into con_probe default values;" >/dev/null \
  || fail "write through pg-primary failed"
pass "write through pg-primary works"

ro=$(q pg-replica "select pg_is_in_recovery()") || fail "cannot connect through pg-replica"
[ "$ro" = "t" ] || fail "pg-replica is not a replica: $ro"
pass "pg-replica reaches a replica (pg_is_in_recovery = t)"

if q pg-replica "create table should_fail(i int)" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  fail "write through pg-replica was accepted"
fi
pass "write through pg-replica is rejected"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/rep-topology.sh tests/con-endpoints.sh
shellcheck scripts/gen-secrets.sh scripts/pg-up.sh tests/rep-topology.sh tests/con-endpoints.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"
ls k8s/pg/ scripts/ tests/
shellcheck OK should print, and ls should show all the files.

7. Validate the manifests
kubeconform -strict -summary -ignore-missing-schemas -kubernetes-version 1.37.0 k8s/pg/ \
  || kubeconform -strict -summary -ignore-missing-schemas -kubernetes-version 1.34.0 k8s/pg/
The fallback only changes the schema version. A real error in a manifest still fails both runs, so if you see one, send it to me.

2. Next: replication tests for REP-2, REP-3 and REP-4
These are structural tests that run against the live cluster:

REP-2: a sync standby exists, and it had flushed the WAL by the time a commit returned.
REP-3: a slot exists for each replica, none is lagging, and the disk cap is set.
REP-4: lag in bytes and time is queried from the primary.
The part of REP-3 that proves a stopped replica can’t fill the disk is a destructive drill. I’ll do it as a separate step with a lowered limit, so it can’t fill your PVC.

tee tests/rep-sync.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# REP-2: synchronous replication with one sync standby.
set -euo pipefail
NS=pg-ha
pass() { echo "PASS: $*"; }
fail() { echo "FAIL: $*"; exit 1; }

leader=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pods -l application=pg-ha,role=primary -o jsonpath='{.items[0].metadata.name}')
sql() { kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c "$1"; }

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
echo "leader: $leader"

names=$(sql "show synchronous_standby_names")
echo "synchronous_standby_names = $names"
[ -n "$names" ] || fail "synchronous_standby_names is empty"
pass "synchronous_standby_names is set"

[ "$(sql "show synchronous_commit")" = "on" ] || fail "synchronous_commit is not on"
pass "synchronous_commit = on"

count=$(sql "select count(*) from pg_stat_replication where sync_state = 'sync'")
[ "$count" = 1 ] || fail "expected 1 sync standby, found $count"
pass "exactly 1 sync standby in pg_stat_replication"

sql "create table if not exists sync_probe(i int)" >/dev/null
# In one session: note the WAL position before COMMIT, commit, then check the
# sync standby had already flushed at least that position when COMMIT returned.
flushed=$(kubectl -n "$NS" exec -i "$leader" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 <<'SQL'
begin;
insert into sync_probe values (1);
select pg_current_wal_insert_lsn() as pre_commit_lsn \gset
commit;
select count(*) from pg_stat_replication where sync_state = 'sync' and flush_lsn >= :'pre_commit_lsn';
SQL
)
[ "$flushed" = 1 ] || fail "sync standby had not flushed the commit when COMMIT returned"
pass "sync standby had flushed the WAL when COMMIT returned"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF

tee tests/rep-slots.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# REP-3: replication slots exist and are healthy; WAL retention is capped.
set -euo pipefail
NS=pg-ha
pass() { echo "PASS: $*"; }
fail() { echo "FAIL: $*"; exit 1; }

leader=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pods -l application=pg-ha,role=primary -o jsonpath='{.items[0].metadata.name}')
sql() { kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c "$1"; }

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
echo "leader: $leader"
sql "select slot_name, slot_type, active, wal_status from pg_replication_slots order by 1"

active=$(sql "select count(*) from pg_replication_slots where slot_type = 'physical' and active")
[ "$active" = 2 ] || fail "expected 2 active physical slots, found $active"
pass "2 active physical slots (one per replica)"

bad=$(sql "select count(*) from pg_replication_slots where wal_status <> 'reserved'")
[ "$bad" = 0 ] || fail "$bad slot(s) not in wal_status 'reserved'"
pass "all slots have wal_status = reserved"

cap=$(sql "show max_slot_wal_keep_size")
echo "max_slot_wal_keep_size = $cap"
[ "$cap" = "1GB" ] || fail "max_slot_wal_keep_size is $cap, expected 1GB"
pass "slot WAL retention is capped at 1GB"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF

tee tests/rep-lag.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# REP-4: replication lag is measured, in bytes and in time.
set -euo pipefail
NS=pg-ha
pass() { echo "PASS: $*"; }
fail() { echo "FAIL: $*"; exit 1; }

leader=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pods -l application=pg-ha,role=primary -o jsonpath='{.items[0].metadata.name}')
sql() { kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c "$1"; }

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
echo "leader: $leader"
sql "create table if not exists sync_probe(i int)" >/dev/null
sql "insert into sync_probe values (2)" >/dev/null

echo "replica | replay_lag_bytes | replay_lag_time"
rows=$(sql "select application_name, pg_wal_lsn_diff(pg_current_wal_lsn(), replay_lsn)::bigint, coalesce(replay_lag::text, 'n/a') from pg_stat_replication order by 1")
echo "$rows"

n=$(grep -c . <<<"$rows")
[ "$n" = 2 ] || fail "expected lag rows for 2 replicas, got $n"
pass "lag reported for 2 replicas"

while IFS='|' read -r name bytes _; do
  [[ "$bytes" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || fail "lag in bytes for $name is not a number: $bytes"
done <<<"$rows"
pass "lag in bytes is a number for every replica"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/rep-sync.sh tests/rep-slots.sh tests/rep-lag.sh
shellcheck tests/rep-sync.sh tests/rep-slots.sh tests/rep-lag.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"

msmiraz@HMSMIRAZ:~/postgres-ha-lab$ git status --short git add -A git commit -m "feat: patroni statefulset, services, rbac, pdb with replication and endpoint tests" git push M docs/evidence/IMG-build.txt M image/Dockerfile ?? docs/evidence/CON-1-2.txt ?? docs/evidence/REP-1.txt ?? k8s/pg/

PASTED
Everything passed, and all three commits are pushed (43849d3, 4464da6). These are the results:

REP-2: pg-ha-0 is the sync standby, and it had flushed the WAL by the time COMMIT returned.
REP-3: two active physical slots, both reserved, and the WAL cap is 1 GB.
REP-4: replay lag is 0 bytes and about 3 ms on both replicas.
Phase 2, step 4: the first failover test (FO-1)
How the RTO is measured:

A probe pod runs on the control-plane node, so it survives worker failures. About 10 times a second it inserts a row with a timestamp through the pg-primary service and logs ok <ms> or err <ms>.
RTO is the largest gap between consecutive acknowledged writes. The test also reports the time from the kill to the first successful write.
The test then checks that every acknowledged write is still in the table afterwards. That is the evidence for the RPO claim in ADR-0001.
What to expect: the kill is a force delete with grace period 0, which is a crash, so Patroni never releases the leader lock. Two outcomes are possible:

A replica is promoted after the 30 s lock TTL.
The same pod restarts within the TTL and keeps its lock.
The test records which one happened and accepts either, because both are real Patroni behaviour. Node loss (FO-2) is the test that forces a promotion, since the pod can’t come back there.

1. Create the probe pod and shared helpers
mkdir -p tests/lib
tee tests/lib/write-probe.yaml > /dev/null <<'EOF'
apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: write-probe
  namespace: pg-ha
  labels:
    app: write-probe
spec:
  restartPolicy: Never
  # Run on the control-plane node so worker failures do not kill the probe.
  nodeSelector:
    node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane: ""
  tolerations:
    - key: node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane
      operator: Exists
      effect: NoSchedule
  securityContext:
    runAsNonRoot: true
    runAsUser: 999
    runAsGroup: 999
    seccompProfile:
      type: RuntimeDefault
  containers:
    - name: probe
      image: __IMAGE__
      imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent
      command: ["bash", "-c"]
      args:
        - |
          while true; do
            ts=$(date +%s%3N)
            if PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT=1 PGOPTIONS='-c statement_timeout=3000' timeout 4 \
                 psql -h pg-primary -U app -d appdb -Atq \
                 -c "insert into fo_probe(ts_ms) values ($ts)" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
              echo "ok $ts"
            else
              echo "err $ts"
            fi
            sleep 0.1
          done
      env:
        - name: PGPASSWORD
          valueFrom:
            secretKeyRef:
              name: pg-credentials
              key: app-password
      securityContext:
        allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
        capabilities:
          drop: ["ALL"]
      resources:
        requests:
          cpu: 50m
          memory: 64Mi
        limits:
          memory: 128Mi
EOF

tee tests/lib/probe.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Shared helpers for failover tests. Source this file, do not run it.
export LC_ALL=C
NS=pg-ha
PROBE=write-probe
LIBDIR="$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")"

pass() { echo "PASS: $*"; }
fail() { echo "FAIL: $*"; exit 1; }
now_ms() { date +%s%3N; }

# Run SQL through the pg-primary service as the app user, from any pod that answers.
sql_primary() {
  local pod out
  for pod in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    # Single quotes are intended: the variables expand inside the pod.
    # shellcheck disable=SC2016
    if out=$(kubectl -n "$NS" exec -i "$pod" -c postgres -- sh -c \
      'PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT=2 PGPASSWORD="$APP_PASSWORD" psql -h pg-primary -U app -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1' \
      <<<"$1" 2>/dev/null); then
      echo "$out"
      return 0
    fi
  done
  return 1
}

# Patroni's view of the cluster, from any pod that answers.
cluster_json() {
  local pod
  for pod in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$pod" -c postgres -- \
      patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list -f json 2>/dev/null && return 0
  done
  return 1
}

cluster_table() {
  local pod
  for pod in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$pod" -c postgres -- \
      patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list 2>/dev/null && return 0
  done
  return 1
}

# Wait until Patroni reports 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas. $1 = timeout in seconds.
wait_healthy() {
  local deadline=$((SECONDS + $1)) state l f
  while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
    state=$(cluster_json || true)
    l=$(jq '[.[] | select(.Role == "Leader")] | length' <<<"$state" 2>/dev/null || true)
    f=$(jq '[.[] | select((.Role == "Replica" or .Role == "Sync Standby") and .State == "streaming")] | length' <<<"$state" 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "$l" = 1 ] && [ "$f" = 2 ]; then return 0; fi
    sleep 3
  done
  return 1
}

current_leader() { cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .Member'; }

# Number of pods that currently accept writes (must never be more than 1).
writable_primaries() {
  local pod n=0 r
  for pod in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    r=$(kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$pod" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atqc "select pg_is_in_recovery()" 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "$r" = "f" ]; then n=$((n + 1)); fi
  done
  echo "$n"
}

start_probe() {
  local img
  # shellcheck source=/dev/null
  source "$LIBDIR/../../versions.env"
  img="${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}"
  sql_primary "create table if not exists fo_probe(id bigserial primary key, ts_ms bigint not null); truncate fo_probe;" >/dev/null \
    || fail "cannot prepare the fo_probe table"
  kubectl -n "$NS" delete pod "$PROBE" --ignore-not-found --wait=true >/dev/null
  sed "s|__IMAGE__|${img}|g" "$LIBDIR/write-probe.yaml" | kubectl apply -f - >/dev/null
  kubectl -n "$NS" wait --for=condition=Ready "pod/$PROBE" --timeout=120s >/dev/null \
    || fail "probe pod did not become ready"
  sleep 5
}

# $1 = log file, $2 = kill timestamp (ms). Prints: oks errs max_gap_ms time_to_first_write_ms
analyze_probe() {
  local f=$1 kill=$2 oks errs max_gap first
  oks=$(grep -c '^ok ' "$f" || true)
  errs=$(grep -c '^err ' "$f" || true)
  max_gap=$(awk '$1=="ok" { if (prev) { g=$2-prev; if (g>max) max=g } prev=$2 } END { print max+0 }' "$f")
  first=$(awk -v k="$kill" '$1=="ok" && $2>k {print $2; exit}' "$f")
  echo "$oks $errs $max_gap $((first - kill))"
}

# $1 = log file. Prints how many acknowledged writes are missing from the table.
acked_missing() {
  local acked rows n
  acked=$(mktemp)
  rows=$(mktemp)
  awk '$1=="ok" {print $2}' "$1" | sort -u > "$acked"
  sql_primary "select ts_ms from fo_probe" | sort -u > "$rows"
  n=$(comm -23 "$acked" "$rows" | wc -l)
  rm -f "$acked" "$rows"
  echo "$n"
}
EOF
2. Write the FO-1 test
tee tests/fo-pod-kill.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# FO-1: a crashed primary pod is recovered automatically. Measures RTO.
# RTO = largest gap between consecutive acknowledged writes through pg-primary.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

TARGET_RTO_S=60
LOG=$(mktemp)
trap 'kubectl -n "$NS" delete pod "$PROBE" --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true; rm -f "$LOG"' EXIT

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"
old=$(current_leader)
echo "leader before: $old"

start_probe
before=$(kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$PROBE" | grep -c '^ok ' || true)
[ "$before" -ge 20 ] || fail "probe is not writing before the kill ($before acknowledged writes)"
pass "probe writing before the kill ($before acknowledged writes)"

kill_ms=$(now_ms)
echo "killing $old (force delete, grace period 0) at $kill_ms"
kubectl -n "$NS" delete pod "$old" --grace-period=0 --force >/dev/null 2>&1

deadline=$((SECONDS + 180))
until kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$PROBE" > "$LOG" && awk -v k="$kill_ms" '$1=="ok" && $2>k {f=1} END {exit !f}' "$LOG"; do
  [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ] || fail "writes did not resume within 180 s"
  sleep 1
done
pass "writes through pg-primary resumed without any manual change"

wait_healthy 300 || fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"
new=$(current_leader)
echo "leader after: $new"
if [ "$new" = "$old" ]; then
  echo "NOTE: $old restarted in place and kept its leader lock (no promotion)"
else
  echo "NOTE: replica $new was promoted"
fi
cluster_table
pass "cluster is back to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"

sleep 10
kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$PROBE" > "$LOG"

read -r oks errs max_gap ttr < <(analyze_probe "$LOG" "$kill_ms")
echo "acknowledged writes: $oks, failed attempts: $errs"
echo "time from kill to first successful write: ${ttr} ms"
echo "largest gap between acknowledged writes (RTO): ${max_gap} ms"
[ "$max_gap" -le $((TARGET_RTO_S * 1000)) ] || fail "RTO ${max_gap} ms is above the ${TARGET_RTO_S} s target"
pass "RTO ${max_gap} ms is within the ${TARGET_RTO_S} s target"

missing=$(acked_missing "$LOG")
[ "$missing" = 0 ] || fail "$missing acknowledged write(s) are missing after failover"
pass "no acknowledged write was lost (0 missing out of $oks)"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/fo-pod-kill.sh tests/lib/probe.sh
shellcheck tests/lib/probe.sh tests/fo-pod-kill.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"
kubeconform -strict -ignore-missing-schemas -kubernetes-version 1.34.0 tests/lib/write-probe.yaml
The probe YAML contains the __IMAGE__ placeholder inside an image field, which is valid as a string, so kubeconform should accept it.

PASTED
FO-1 passed and the evidence is committed (7cec591). Here’s what it shows:

RTO was 6.7 s. The writes were interrupted from the kill until the first success at 6.4 s.
No acknowledged write was lost: 0 missing out of 105.
Exactly one writable primary at the end.
No promotion happened. pg-ha-1 restarted in place and kept its leader lock. The kubelet restarts a killed pod in a few seconds, which is faster than the 30 s lock TTL, so Patroni never needed to elect anyone. The timeline went from 1 to 2, and I’m recording that as an observation without claiming why.
So FO-1 proves automatic recovery and a stable endpoint, but it doesn’t prove promotion. FO-2 forces that, because a dead node can’t restart its pod.

Phase 2, step 5: node loss (FO-2)
What the test does:

It finds the node running the primary and powers it off with docker kill. This is abrupt, like a power cut. docker stop would shut the node down gracefully, so Patroni would stop Postgres cleanly and could release the lock, which wouldn’t be a real failure.
It measures the RTO with the same write probe, which runs on the control-plane node and survives.
It requires that a different pod becomes the leader.
It then starts the node again and requires the old primary to rejoin as a replica with no manual step. That covers FO-5 for this case.
What to expect: a longer RTO than FO-1. Patroni needs the 30 s lock TTL to expire, and Kubernetes keeps the dead pod’s endpoint for roughly 40 s until the node is marked NotReady. Both delays are part of the measured result. The target is 90 s.

1. Add a timeout to the shared helpers
Now a dead node is part of the test, and kubectl exec into a pod on a dead node can hang. This wraps every exec in a 20 s limit:

sed -i 's/kubectl -n "\$NS" exec/timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec/g' tests/lib/probe.sh
grep -c 'timeout 20 kubectl' tests/lib/probe.sh
shellcheck tests/lib/probe.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"
The grep should print 6, and shellcheck OK should follow.

2. Write the FO-2 test
tee tests/fo-node-stop.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# FO-2: the node running the primary is powered off. A replica must be promoted
# automatically, and the old primary must rejoin as a replica when its node returns.
# RTO = largest gap between consecutive acknowledged writes through pg-primary.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

TARGET_RTO_S=90
LOG=$(mktemp)
node=""

cleanup() {
  kubectl -n "$NS" delete pod "$PROBE" --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  if [ -n "$node" ] && [ "$(docker inspect -f '{{.State.Running}}' "$node" 2>/dev/null || echo false)" != "true" ]; then
    echo "cleanup: starting $node again"
    docker start "$node" >/dev/null || true
  fi
  rm -f "$LOG"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"
old=$(current_leader)
node=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$old" -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
echo "leader before: $old on node $node"
tl_before=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')

start_probe
before=$(kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$PROBE" | grep -c '^ok ' || true)
[ "$before" -ge 20 ] || fail "probe is not writing before the failure ($before acknowledged writes)"
pass "probe writing before the failure ($before acknowledged writes)"

kill_ms=$(now_ms)
echo "powering off node $node (docker kill) at $kill_ms"
docker kill "$node" >/dev/null

deadline=$((SECONDS + 240))
until kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$PROBE" > "$LOG" && awk -v k="$kill_ms" '$1=="ok" && $2>k {f=1} END {exit !f}' "$LOG"; do
  [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ] || fail "writes did not resume within 240 s"
  sleep 1
done
pass "writes through pg-primary resumed without any manual change"

new=""
deadline=$((SECONDS + 120))
while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
  new=$(current_leader 2>/dev/null || true)
  if [ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$old" ]; then break; fi
  sleep 3
done
echo "leader after: $new"
[ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$old" ] || fail "no replica was promoted (leader is still '$new')"
pass "replica $new was promoted automatically (the old primary's node is down)"

sleep 10
kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$PROBE" > "$LOG"
read -r oks errs max_gap ttr < <(analyze_probe "$LOG" "$kill_ms")
echo "acknowledged writes: $oks, failed attempts: $errs"
echo "time from node power-off to first successful write: ${ttr} ms"
echo "largest gap between acknowledged writes (RTO): ${max_gap} ms"
[ "$max_gap" -le $((TARGET_RTO_S * 1000)) ] || fail "RTO ${max_gap} ms is above the ${TARGET_RTO_S} s target"
pass "RTO ${max_gap} ms is within the ${TARGET_RTO_S} s target"

missing=$(acked_missing "$LOG")
[ "$missing" = 0 ] || fail "$missing acknowledged write(s) are missing after failover"
pass "no acknowledged write was lost (0 missing out of $oks)"

echo "starting node $node again"
docker start "$node" >/dev/null
wait_healthy 600 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table
role=$(cluster_json | jq -r --arg m "$old" '.[] | select(.Member == $m) | .Role')
echo "old primary $old now has role: $role"
case "$role" in
  Replica|"Sync Standby") pass "old primary $old rejoined automatically as a replica" ;;
  *) fail "old primary $old has unexpected role '$role'" ;;
esac

tl_after=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')
echo "timeline before: $tl_before, after: $tl_after"
[ "$tl_after" -gt "$tl_before" ] || fail "timeline did not advance"
pass "timeline advanced ($tl_before -> $tl_after)"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes after the node returned"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/fo-node-stop.sh
shellcheck tests/fo-node-stop.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"

PASTED
FO-2 failed. A replica was promoted automatically and the RTO was 25.6 s, within the 90 s target, but the data check says all 44 acknowledged writes are missing. That output is evidence, so we won’t hide it. Here is what I know, and what I don’t yet.

It’s probably a flaw in my test, but I haven’t proven that. Thirty of those 44 writes were acknowledged before the kill, while the sync standby was pg-ha-2, the pod that was then promoted. Losing all of them would contradict ADR-0001, so a real loss is unlikely.
The likely cause is my helper. It reads the table with sql_primary, and inside a command substitution bash turns set -e off. If that read failed, for example while pg-primary was still settling, the table looked empty and every write counted as missing. FO-1 had no failed read, so it didn’t show this.
Either way the test is wrong. It conflates “couldn’t read the table” with “all writes lost”. The fix is to retry the read, to report an unreadable table as its own error, and to save the probe log so the result can be checked by hand.
My earlier count was wrong. I said grep -c would print 6, and the correct number is 4, so your result was right.
1. Check the cluster recovered after the node restart
kubectl get nodes
kubectl -n pg-ha get pods -o wide
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list
You should see all 4 nodes Ready (a node can be NotReady for a few minutes after the restart), 3 pods Running, and 1 leader with 2 streaming replicas. Wait for that before the rerun.

3. Fix the test helper and save the probe log
tee -a tests/lib/probe.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'

# Like acked_missing, but retries the table read and says so when it cannot read at all.
# Prints the number of missing acknowledged writes, or "unreadable". Diagnostics go to stderr.
acked_missing_verbose() {
  local acked rows raw ok=0 i n
  acked=$(mktemp); rows=$(mktemp); raw=$(mktemp)
  awk '$1=="ok" {print $2}' "$1" | sort -u > "$acked"
  for i in $(seq 1 12); do
    if sql_primary "select ts_ms from fo_probe" > "$raw"; then ok=1; break; fi
    echo "DIAG: could not read fo_probe (attempt $i), retrying in 5 s" >&2
    sleep 5
  done
  if [ "$ok" != 1 ]; then
    echo "DIAG: fo_probe could not be read at all" >&2
    rm -f "$acked" "$rows" "$raw"
    echo "unreadable"
    return 0
  fi
  sort -u "$raw" > "$rows"
  echo "DIAG: acknowledged in log: $(wc -l < "$acked"), rows read from table: $(wc -l < "$rows")" >&2
  echo "DIAG: first missing: $(comm -23 "$acked" "$rows" | head -3 | tr '\n' ' ')" >&2
  n=$(comm -23 "$acked" "$rows" | wc -l)
  rm -f "$acked" "$rows" "$raw"
  echo "$n"
}
EOF
sed -i 's|missing=\$(acked_missing "\$LOG")|cp "$LOG" docs/evidence/FO-2-probe.log\nmissing=$(acked_missing_verbose "$LOG")|' tests/fo-node-stop.sh
grep -n "acked_missing_verbose\|FO-2-probe.log" tests/fo-node-stop.sh
shellcheck tests/lib/probe.sh tests/fo-node-stop.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"
The grep should show two matching lines in fo-node-stop.sh.

# Like acked_missing, but retries the table read and says so when it cannot read at all.
# Prints the number of missing acknowledged writes, or "unreadable". Diagnostics go to stderr.
acked_missing_verbose() {
  local acked rows raw ok=0 i n
  acked=$(mktemp); rows=$(mktemp); raw=$(mktemp)
  awk '$1=="ok" {print $2}' "$1" | sort -u > "$acked"
  for i in $(seq 1 12); do
    if sql_primary "select ts_ms from fo_probe" > "$raw"; then ok=1; break; fi
    echo "DIAG: could not read fo_probe (attempt $i), retrying in 5 s" >&2
    sleep 5
  done
  if [ "$ok" != 1 ]; then
    echo "DIAG: fo_probe could not be read at all" >&2
    rm -f "$acked" "$rows" "$raw"
    echo "unreadable"
    return 0
  fi
  sort -u "$raw" > "$rows"
  echo "DIAG: acknowledged in log: $(wc -l < "$acked"), rows read from table: $(wc -l < "$rows")" >&2
  echo "DIAG: first missing: $(comm -23 "$acked" "$rows" | head -3 | tr '\n' ' ')" >&2
  n=$(comm -23 "$acked" "$rows" | wc -l)
  rm -f "$acked" "$rows" "$raw"
  echo "$n"
}
EOF
sed -i 's|missing=\$(acked_missing "\$LOG")|cp "$LOG" docs/evidence/FO-2-probe.log\nmissing=$(acked_missing_verbose "$LOG")|' tests/fo-node-stop.sh
grep -n "acked_missing_verbose\|FO-2-probe.log" tests/fo-node-stop.sh
shellcheck tests/lib/probe.sh tests/fo-node-stop.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"
67:cp "$LOG" docs/evidence/FO-2-probe.log
68:missing=$(acked_missing_verbose "$LOG")
shellcheck OK

FO-2 passed, and I read the result as follows.

A replica was promoted automatically. pg-ha-2 was on the powered-off node, pg-ha-0 became leader, and the timeline went from 3 to 4.
RTO was 33.4 s. That matches the design: the 30 s lock TTL plus a few seconds of detection. It’s well within the 90 s target, and it’s the number to quote for node loss.
No acknowledged write was lost. All 56 are in the table. The table holds 62 rows, so 6 writes committed but the probe never got an acknowledgement before the node died. That’s normal and harmless: those commits happened, only the reply was lost.
The old primary rejoined by itself as a replica on timeline 4, with exactly one writable primary.
Two caveats go in the findings document:

The first run’s failure is still unexplained. Run 2 passed with the more careful read and showed no retry messages. My best guess is still a read problem in my first helper, but I never saved the first run’s probe log, so I can’t prove it. I’m keeping FO-2-run1-FAILED.txt in the repo and will say exactly that.
The line “time from node power-off to first successful write: 107 ms” is an artifact. It’s a probe attempt that started a few milliseconds before the node went down. Ignore that line and use the largest-gap RTO. The next test leaves the line out.
Phase 2, step 6: storage loss (FO-3)
What the test does:

It deletes the primary’s PVC and force-deletes its pod.
It checks that the PVC UID changed, which proves the storage was really replaced and not just restarted.
It requires that a different pod becomes leader, that the old pod comes back as a replica (re-cloned from the new leader), and that no acknowledged write is lost.
The cluster uses kind’s default local-path storage, so deleting the PVC deletes the data on that node’s disk.

1. Write the FO-3 test
tee tests/fo-pvc-loss.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# FO-3: the primary's storage is lost (PVC deleted together with the pod).
# A replica must be promoted, and the old pod must come back with fresh storage
# and rejoin as a replica by re-cloning, with no manual step.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

TARGET_RTO_S=90
LOG=$(mktemp)
trap 'kubectl -n "$NS" delete pod "$PROBE" --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true; rm -f "$LOG"' EXIT

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"
old=$(current_leader)
pvc="data-${old}"
uid_before=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pvc "$pvc" -o jsonpath='{.metadata.uid}')
tl_before=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')
echo "leader before: $old, pvc $pvc, uid $uid_before, timeline $tl_before"

start_probe
before=$(kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$PROBE" | grep -c '^ok ' || true)
[ "$before" -ge 20 ] || fail "probe is not writing before the failure ($before acknowledged writes)"
pass "probe writing before the failure ($before acknowledged writes)"

kill_ms=$(now_ms)
echo "deleting pvc $pvc and force-deleting pod $old at $kill_ms"
kubectl -n "$NS" delete pvc "$pvc" --wait=false >/dev/null
kubectl -n "$NS" delete pod "$old" --grace-period=0 --force >/dev/null 2>&1

deadline=$((SECONDS + 240))
until kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$PROBE" > "$LOG" && awk -v k="$kill_ms" '$1=="ok" && $2>k+2000 {f=1} END {exit !f}' "$LOG"; do
  [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ] || fail "writes did not resume within 240 s"
  sleep 1
done
pass "writes through pg-primary resumed without any manual change"

new=""
deadline=$((SECONDS + 120))
while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
  new=$(current_leader 2>/dev/null || true)
  if [ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$old" ]; then break; fi
  sleep 3
done
echo "leader after: $new"
[ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$old" ] || fail "no replica was promoted (leader is still '$new')"
pass "replica $new was promoted automatically"

wait_healthy 600 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table

uid_after=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pvc "$pvc" -o jsonpath='{.metadata.uid}')
echo "pvc uid after: $uid_after"
[ "$uid_after" != "$uid_before" ] || fail "the PVC was not replaced (same uid)"
pass "storage was really replaced (new PVC uid)"

role=$(cluster_json | jq -r --arg m "$old" '.[] | select(.Member == $m) | .Role')
echo "old primary $old now has role: $role"
case "$role" in
  Replica|"Sync Standby") pass "old pod $old re-cloned and rejoined as a replica" ;;
  *) fail "old pod $old has unexpected role '$role'" ;;
esac
echo "--- how $old got its data (from its log) ---"
kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$old" -c postgres 2>/dev/null | grep -iE "bootstrap|basebackup|clone|empty|replica" | head -8 || true

sleep 10
kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$PROBE" > "$LOG"
cp "$LOG" docs/evidence/FO-3-probe.log
read -r oks errs max_gap _ < <(analyze_probe "$LOG" "$kill_ms")
echo "acknowledged writes: $oks, failed attempts: $errs"
echo "largest gap between acknowledged writes (RTO): ${max_gap} ms"
[ "$max_gap" -le $((TARGET_RTO_S * 1000)) ] || fail "RTO ${max_gap} ms is above the ${TARGET_RTO_S} s target"
pass "RTO ${max_gap} ms is within the ${TARGET_RTO_S} s target"

missing=$(acked_missing_verbose "$LOG")
[ "$missing" = 0 ] || fail "acknowledged writes missing after failover: $missing"
pass "no acknowledged write was lost (0 missing out of $oks)"

tl_after=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')
echo "timeline before: $tl_before, after: $tl_after"
[ "$tl_after" -gt "$tl_before" ] || fail "timeline did not advance"
pass "timeline advanced ($tl_before -> $tl_after)"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/fo-pvc-loss.sh
shellcheck tests/fo-pvc-loss.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"
2. Run it
It takes about 3 to 6 minutes. Don’t press anything while it runs:

bash tests/fo-pvc-loss.sh 2>&1 | tee docs/evidence/FO-3.txt
3. Commit
Commit it whatever the result, so the evidence is complete:

git add -A
git commit -m "test: storage-loss failover test (PVC deleted) with re-clone and RTO measurement"
git push
If something goes wrong
The new pod stays Pending: a pod can’t start while its PVC is still terminating. Run kubectl -n pg-ha get pods,pvc and send me the output. Don’t delete anything.
The old pod keeps the leader lock and doesn’t go back to a replica: that’s a real finding about how Patroni treats an empty data directory. Send me the output and kubectl -n pg-ha logs <old pod> -c postgres --tail=40.
Send me
The full output of the test. I’m looking for the leader change, the PVC UIDs, the role of the old pod, and the log lines showing how it got its data back.

After this, FO-4 (split-brain) and FO-5 (the rejoin proof) are next. FO-5 is already partly covered by FO-2 and FO-3.


hmsmiraz@HMSMIRAZ:~/postgres-ha-lab$ tee tests/fo-pvc-loss.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF' #!/usr/bin/env bash # FO-3: the primary's storage is lost (PVC deleted together with the pod). # A replica must be promoted, and the old pod must come back with fresh storage # and rejoin as a replica by re-cloning

PASTED
FO-3 passed and the evidence is committed (2905746). These are the results:

A replica was promoted: pg-ha-1 took over, and the timeline went from 4 to 5.
The storage was really replaced. The PVC UID changed, and pg-ha-0 came back empty and re-cloned itself from the new leader.
RTO was 8.0 s, with 0 of 157 acknowledged writes missing.
The log excerpt shows two things to put in the write-up:

Why 8 s and not 30+ s. The log says released leader key voluntarily as data dir empty and currently leader. Patroni saw its own empty data directory and gave up the lock itself, so no TTL had to expire. RTO depends on the failure: 6.7 s for a pod crash, 8 s for storage loss, and 33.4 s for node loss. Node loss is the worst case and the one to quote.
A transient failure that healed itself. The first clone attempt failed with the standby was promoted during online backup because it raced the promotion. Patroni retried and the second attempt succeeded. I’ll mention it in the findings.
Phase 2, step 7: split-brain (FO-4)
This is the test that matters most for a reviewer. The scenario is a primary that is alive but cut off from the Kubernetes API, where the lock can’t be renewed.

How the test creates it:

It adds a Calico egress NetworkPolicy to the primary pod that blocks only the API server’s IP, and it checks from inside the pod that the API is really unreachable.
It samples all three pods every second and records which ones accept writes. Passing means no sampled moment had two writable primaries.
It tries a direct write to the isolated old primary after the new leader is up, which must fail.
It removes the policy and checks that the old primary rejoins as a replica, with no acknowledged write lost.
What I’m watching for, as a prediction and not a result. The isolated pod can’t update its own role label. If the label stays primary, the pg-primary service may briefly list both pods, and the probe could see write errors from the demoted one. That would be an availability glitch and not split-brain, and the test will show if it happens.

A limit I’ll state in the output. The three samples in a round aren’t taken at the same instant (about 0.3 s apart). The test proves no sustained overlap, and the actual guarantee comes from the design in ADR-0003.

1. Write the FO-4 test
tee tests/fo-split-brain.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# FO-4: the primary is alive but cut off from the Kubernetes API (network partition).
# It must demote itself, a replica must be promoted, and two writable primaries
# must never be seen at the same time. Then the old primary must rejoin as a replica.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

TARGET_RTO_S=90
LOG=$(mktemp)
SAMPLES=$(mktemp)
sampler_pid=""
EXCLUDE=""

# While a pod is cut off from the API, patronictl inside it cannot answer, so skip it.
cluster_json() {
  local pod
  for pod in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    [ "$pod" = "$EXCLUDE" ] && continue
    timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$pod" -c postgres -- \
      patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list -f json 2>/dev/null && return 0
  done
  return 1
}

cleanup() {
  if [ -n "$sampler_pid" ]; then kill "$sampler_pid" 2>/dev/null || true; fi
  kubectl -n "$NS" delete networkpolicy block-api-egress --ignore-not-found >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  kubectl -n "$NS" delete pod "$PROBE" --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  rm -f "$LOG" "$SAMPLES"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

# One line per pod per second: "<ms> <round> <pod> <f|t|x>" (f = accepts writes, t = read-only, x = no answer).
sampler() {
  local round=0 pod
  while true; do
    round=$((round + 1))
    for pod in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
      (
        r=$(timeout 3 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$pod" -c postgres -- \
          psql -U postgres -Atqc "select pg_is_in_recovery()" 2>/dev/null || true)
        echo "$(now_ms) $round $pod ${r:-x}" >> "$SAMPLES"
      ) &
    done
    wait
    sleep 1
  done
}

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"
old=$(current_leader)
tl_before=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')
api_ip=$(kubectl get endpointslices -l kubernetes.io/service-name=kubernetes \
  -o jsonpath='{.items[0].endpoints[0].addresses[0]}')
echo "leader before: $old (timeline $tl_before), API server address: $api_ip"

api_reachable() {
  timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$old" -c postgres -- \
    timeout 4 bash -c "exec 3<>/dev/tcp/${api_ip}/6443" >/dev/null 2>&1
}
api_reachable || fail "method check: the API is not reachable from $old before the partition"
pass "method check: $old can reach the API server before the partition"

start_probe
before=$(kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$PROBE" | grep -c '^ok ' || true)
[ "$before" -ge 20 ] || fail "probe is not writing before the partition ($before acknowledged writes)"
pass "probe writing before the partition ($before acknowledged writes)"

sampler &
sampler_pid=$!
sleep 5

partition_ms=$(now_ms)
echo "cutting $old off from the Kubernetes API at $partition_ms"
kubectl -n "$NS" apply -f - >/dev/null <<YAML
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: block-api-egress
  namespace: $NS
spec:
  podSelector:
    matchLabels:
      statefulset.kubernetes.io/pod-name: $old
  policyTypes: ["Egress"]
  egress:
    - to:
        - ipBlock:
            cidr: 0.0.0.0/0
            except: ["${api_ip}/32"]
YAML
sleep 5
if api_reachable; then fail "the partition did not work: $old can still reach the API"; fi
pass "$old can no longer reach the Kubernetes API"
EXCLUDE="$old"

new=""
deadline=$((SECONDS + 180))
while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
  new=$(current_leader 2>/dev/null || true)
  if [ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$old" ]; then break; fi
  sleep 3
done
echo "leader after: $new"
[ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$old" ] || fail "no replica was promoted while $old was partitioned"
pass "replica $new was promoted automatically"

fenced=""
deadline=$((SECONDS + 90))
while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
  r=$(timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$old" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atqc "select pg_is_in_recovery()" 2>/dev/null || true)
  if [ "$r" = "t" ]; then fenced=yes; break; fi
  sleep 3
done
[ "$fenced" = yes ] || fail "the partitioned old primary $old is still accepting writes"
pass "the partitioned old primary $old demoted itself (now read-only)"

if timeout 25 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$old" -c postgres -- \
     psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 \
     -c "set statement_timeout = 3000" -c "insert into fo_probe(ts_ms) values (-1)" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  fail "a direct write to the old primary $old was accepted (split-brain)"
fi
pass "a direct write to the old primary $old is rejected"

echo "healing the partition"
kubectl -n "$NS" delete networkpolicy block-api-egress >/dev/null
EXCLUDE=""
wait_healthy 600 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table
role=$(cluster_json | jq -r --arg m "$old" '.[] | select(.Member == $m) | .Role')
echo "old primary $old now has role: $role"
case "$role" in
  Replica|"Sync Standby") pass "old primary $old rejoined as a replica after the partition healed" ;;
  *) fail "old primary $old has unexpected role '$role'" ;;
esac
echo "--- rewind-related lines from $old (informational) ---"
kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$old" -c postgres 2>/dev/null | grep -iE "rewind|diverg|demot|timeline" | tail -8 || true

sleep 10
kill "$sampler_pid" 2>/dev/null || true
sampler_pid=""
cp "$SAMPLES" docs/evidence/FO-4-samples.log
kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$PROBE" > "$LOG"
cp "$LOG" docs/evidence/FO-4-probe.log

rounds=$(awk '{print $2}' "$SAMPLES" | sort -u | wc -l)
max_writable=$(awk '$4=="f" {c[$2]++} END {m=0; for (r in c) if (c[r]>m) m=c[r]; print m}' "$SAMPLES")
echo "NOTE: pods are sampled about 0.3 s apart within a round, so this shows no sustained overlap."
echo "sampling rounds: $rounds, most writable pods seen in one round: $max_writable"
[ "$max_writable" -le 1 ] || fail "two writable primaries were seen in the same sampling round"
pass "never more than one writable primary in $rounds sampling rounds"

demote_ms=$(awk -v p="$partition_ms" -v o="$old" '$3==o && $4=="t" && $1>p {print $1; exit}' "$SAMPLES")
promote_ms=$(awk -v p="$partition_ms" -v o="$old" '$3!=o && $4=="f" && $1>p {print $1; exit}' "$SAMPLES")
[ -n "$demote_ms" ] && echo "old primary read-only ${demote_ms:+$((demote_ms - partition_ms)) ms} after the partition started"
[ -n "$promote_ms" ] && echo "new primary writable $((promote_ms - partition_ms)) ms after the partition started"

read -r oks errs max_gap _ < <(analyze_probe "$LOG" "$partition_ms")
echo "acknowledged writes: $oks, failed attempts: $errs"
echo "largest gap between acknowledged writes (RTO): ${max_gap} ms"
[ "$max_gap" -le $((TARGET_RTO_S * 1000)) ] || fail "RTO ${max_gap} ms is above the ${TARGET_RTO_S} s target"
pass "RTO ${max_gap} ms is within the ${TARGET_RTO_S} s target"

missing=$(acked_missing_verbose "$LOG")
[ "$missing" = 0 ] || fail "acknowledged writes missing after the partition: $missing"
pass "no acknowledged write was lost (0 missing out of $oks)"

tl_after=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')
echo "timeline before: $tl_before, after: $tl_after"
[ "$tl_after" -gt "$tl_before" ] || fail "timeline did not advance"
pass "timeline advanced ($tl_before -> $tl_after)"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes at the end"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/fo-split-brain.sh
shellcheck tests/fo-split-brain.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"

What the FO-4 failure shows
The test applied the network block, and my check confirmed pg-ha-1 couldn’t open a new connection to the API server. But pg-ha-1 stayed leader for the full 180 s and nothing was promoted.

One possible cause, not yet confirmed: the block affects only new connections. Calico accepts traffic that belongs to an already established connection before it checks policies. Patroni keeps a long-lived connection to the API server open, so it may have kept renewing the lock over that connection. If so, the partition never really happened, and the flaw is in my test’s method check, which only tried a new connection. The Patroni logs will confirm or reject this.

The network policy was also still listed when you checked, so my cleanup either hadn’t finished or hadn’t removed it. I’ll make the cleanup verify the deletion.

The test will also check that the lock stops being renewed before it continues.
If pg-ha-1 logged DCS errors and tried to demote but the others didn’t promote, the cause is something else, probably the synchronous-mode promotion rules or the lock expiry. I’ll look at the other pods’ logs for that.

The logs answer the question, and the failure is in my test, not in the cluster. For the whole window, pg-ha-1 logged no action. I am (pg-ha-1), the leader with the lock every 10 s. The other two pods logged Lock owner: pg-ha-1 the entire time. There are no DCS errors, no demotion and no retries, so Patroni never noticed the partition. The block was in place for 3 minutes, and the lock was renewed all the way through.

My explanation, which the next run will test:

Cause: Calico network policy only applies to new connections, and Patroni keeps a long-lived connection to the API server open.
My check missed it: it tested a new connection (blocked) and never checked whether the lock was still being renewed.
What I’m changing: the new test drops the traffic at the node level with an iptables rule. That also kills connections that are already open. It then proves the cut works by checking that the lock’s renewTime stops advancing, which is the evidence a partition happened.
Your cleanup worked: the policy is gone (NotFound) and the cluster is healthy on timeline 5. I’m keeping the failed run in the repo as evidence, and it’ll go in the write-up as the first attempt.

1. Replace the FO-4 test
tee tests/fo-split-brain.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# FO-4: the primary is alive but cut off from the Kubernetes API (network partition).
# The cut is an iptables DROP on the node, in both directions, so it also kills
# connections that are already open. It must demote itself, a replica must be promoted,
# and two writable primaries must never be seen at the same time.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

TARGET_RTO_S=90
LOG=$(mktemp)
SAMPLES=$(mktemp)
sampler_pid=""
EXCLUDE=""
cut_done=""
node=""; old_ip=""; api_ip=""

# While a pod is cut off from the API, patronictl inside it cannot answer, so skip it.
cluster_json() {
  local pod
  for pod in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    [ "$pod" = "$EXCLUDE" ] && continue
    timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$pod" -c postgres -- \
      patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list -f json 2>/dev/null && return 0
  done
  return 1
}

cut() {
  docker exec "$node" iptables -w -I FORWARD 1 -s "$old_ip" -d "$api_ip" -j DROP
  docker exec "$node" iptables -w -I FORWARD 1 -s "$api_ip" -d "$old_ip" -j DROP
  cut_done=yes
}
heal() {
  docker exec "$node" iptables -w -D FORWARD -s "$old_ip" -d "$api_ip" -j DROP 2>/dev/null || true
  docker exec "$node" iptables -w -D FORWARD -s "$api_ip" -d "$old_ip" -j DROP 2>/dev/null || true
  cut_done=""
}
renew_time() { kubectl -n "$NS" get configmap pg-ha-leader -o jsonpath='{.metadata.annotations.renewTime}'; }

cleanup() {
  if [ -n "$sampler_pid" ]; then kill "$sampler_pid" 2>/dev/null || true; fi
  if [ -n "$cut_done" ]; then echo "cleanup: removing the partition"; heal; fi
  kubectl -n "$NS" delete pod "$PROBE" --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  rm -f "$LOG" "$SAMPLES"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

# One line per pod per second: "<ms> <round> <pod> <f|t|x>" (f = accepts writes, t = read-only, x = no answer).
sampler() {
  local round=0 pod
  while true; do
    round=$((round + 1))
    for pod in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
      (
        r=$(timeout 3 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$pod" -c postgres -- \
          psql -U postgres -Atqc "select pg_is_in_recovery()" 2>/dev/null || true)
        echo "$(now_ms) $round $pod ${r:-x}" >> "$SAMPLES"
      ) &
    done
    wait
    sleep 1
  done
}

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"
old=$(current_leader)
tl_before=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')
node=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$old" -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
old_ip=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$old" -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
api_ip=$(kubectl get endpointslices -l kubernetes.io/service-name=kubernetes \
  -o jsonpath='{.items[0].endpoints[0].addresses[0]}')
echo "leader before: $old (timeline $tl_before) on node $node, pod IP $old_ip, API server $api_ip"

start_probe
before=$(kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$PROBE" | grep -c '^ok ' || true)
[ "$before" -ge 20 ] || fail "probe is not writing before the partition ($before acknowledged writes)"
pass "probe writing before the partition ($before acknowledged writes)"

sampler &
sampler_pid=$!
sleep 5

partition_ms=$(now_ms)
echo "cutting $old off from the Kubernetes API (iptables DROP on $node) at $partition_ms"
cut
sleep 2
rt1=$(renew_time)
sleep 14
rt2=$(renew_time)
echo "leader lock renewTime at +2 s: $rt1"
echo "leader lock renewTime at +16 s: $rt2"
[ "$rt1" = "$rt2" ] || fail "the partition did not work: $old is still renewing the leader lock"
pass "method check: $old can no longer renew the leader lock (renewTime stopped)"
EXCLUDE="$old"

new=""
deadline=$((SECONDS + 180))
while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
  new=$(current_leader 2>/dev/null || true)
  if [ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$old" ]; then break; fi
  sleep 3
done
echo "leader after: $new"
[ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$old" ] || fail "no replica was promoted while $old was partitioned"
pass "replica $new was promoted automatically"

fenced=""
deadline=$((SECONDS + 90))
while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
  r=$(timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$old" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atqc "select pg_is_in_recovery()" 2>/dev/null || true)
  if [ "$r" = "t" ]; then fenced=yes; break; fi
  sleep 3
done
[ "$fenced" = yes ] || fail "the partitioned old primary $old is still accepting writes"
pass "the partitioned old primary $old demoted itself (now read-only)"

if timeout 25 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$old" -c postgres -- \
     psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 \
     -c "set statement_timeout = 3000" -c "insert into fo_probe(ts_ms) values (-1)" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  fail "a direct write to the old primary $old was accepted (split-brain)"
fi
pass "a direct write to the old primary $old is rejected"

echo "healing the partition"
heal
EXCLUDE=""
wait_healthy 600 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table
role=$(cluster_json | jq -r --arg m "$old" '.[] | select(.Member == $m) | .Role')
echo "old primary $old now has role: $role"
case "$role" in
  Replica|"Sync Standby") pass "old primary $old rejoined as a replica after the partition healed" ;;
  *) fail "old primary $old has unexpected role '$role'" ;;
esac
echo "--- rewind-related lines from $old (informational) ---"
kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$old" -c postgres 2>/dev/null | grep -iE "rewind|diverg|demot|timeline" | tail -8 || true

sleep 10
kill "$sampler_pid" 2>/dev/null || true
sampler_pid=""
cp "$SAMPLES" docs/evidence/FO-4-samples.log
kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$PROBE" > "$LOG"
cp "$LOG" docs/evidence/FO-4-probe.log

rounds=$(awk '{print $2}' "$SAMPLES" | sort -u | wc -l)
max_writable=$(awk '$4=="f" {c[$2]++} END {m=0; for (r in c) if (c[r]>m) m=c[r]; print m}' "$SAMPLES")
echo "NOTE: pods are sampled about 0.3 s apart within a round, so this shows no sustained overlap."
echo "sampling rounds: $rounds, most writable pods seen in one round: $max_writable"
[ "$max_writable" -le 1 ] || fail "two writable primaries were seen in the same sampling round"
pass "never more than one writable primary in $rounds sampling rounds"

demote_ms=$(awk -v p="$partition_ms" -v o="$old" '$3==o && $4=="t" && $1>p {print $1; exit}' "$SAMPLES")
promote_ms=$(awk -v p="$partition_ms" -v o="$old" '$3!=o && $4=="f" && $1>p {print $1; exit}' "$SAMPLES")
if [ -n "$demote_ms" ]; then echo "old primary read-only $((demote_ms - partition_ms)) ms after the partition started"; fi
if [ -n "$promote_ms" ]; then echo "new primary writable $((promote_ms - partition_ms)) ms after the partition started"; fi

read -r oks errs max_gap _ < <(analyze_probe "$LOG" "$partition_ms")
echo "acknowledged writes: $oks, failed attempts: $errs"
echo "largest gap between acknowledged writes (RTO): ${max_gap} ms"
[ "$max_gap" -le $((TARGET_RTO_S * 1000)) ] || fail "RTO ${max_gap} ms is above the ${TARGET_RTO_S} s target"
pass "RTO ${max_gap} ms is within the ${TARGET_RTO_S} s target"

missing=$(acked_missing_verbose "$LOG")
[ "$missing" = 0 ] || fail "acknowledged writes missing after the partition: $missing"
pass "no acknowledged write was lost (0 missing out of $oks)"

tl_after=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')
echo "timeline before: $tl_before, after: $tl_after"
[ "$tl_after" -gt "$tl_before" ] || fail "timeline did not advance"
pass "timeline advanced ($tl_before -> $tl_after)"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes at the end"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
shellcheck tests/fo-split-brain.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"
