How many steps are left in Phase 2
Four, and FO-4 is no longer one of them:

FO-5, rejoin proof. The FO-4 logs don’t show whether pg_rewind ran, so this test forces a real divergence.
REP-3 drill. A stopped replica must not fill the disk.
ADR-0001 degraded case. What writes do when no sync standby is available.
FT-1 and FT-2 check. Spread across nodes and zones, and the PodDisruptionBudget blocking a node drain.
After that, Phase 2 is closed and Phase 3 (backups) starts.

What the FO-4 result shows
It passed and is committed (192d99d), along with the failed first attempt and both sample logs.

The partition was real. The leader lock’s renewTime stayed frozen at 16:25:48 for the full 16 s check. The first run never cut anything, because the lock kept renewing, so that failure was in my test.
Demote before promote. pg-ha-1 went read-only 15.3 s after the cut. pg-ha-2 became writable at 25.1 s. So the old primary stopped accepting writes about 10 s before the new one started. The sampler saw at most 1 writable pod in all 45 rounds, which is the fencing evidence for ADR-0003.
No data loss: 0 of 324 acknowledged writes missing. Patroni promoted pg-ha-2, which was the sync standby.
RTO was 11.5 s. The probe kept writing to the old primary until it demoted, so the gap is shorter than the 25 s the new primary needed.
The partition was removed completely. All three nodes show 0 leftover iptables rules.
Two limits for the write-up. The three pods are sampled about 0.3 s apart, so the test shows no sustained overlap, not a proof about single instants. The guarantee itself comes from the lock design.

An open question, not a finding. The rewind-related lines block only shows transient errors (Can not fetch local timeline and lsn from replication connection) while the old primary was restarting. It doesn’t say whether pg_rewind ran. My guess is that it wasn’t needed, because the old primary demoted with a clean shutdown and its WAL had already reached the standbys. That’s a hypothesis, and FO-5 tests it.

Phase 2, step 8: FO-5, rejoin after real divergence
The test builds a genuine fork:

It cuts replication between the leader and both replicas with iptables. API access stays up, so the leader keeps its lock.
It writes 5 rows on the leader with synchronous_commit = local, so they commit without waiting for a standby. It checks the rows exist only on the leader.
It powers off the leader’s node, like FO-2. The lock expires and a standby that lacks those rows is promoted.
It restarts the node. The old primary’s history has now diverged, so it needs pg_rewind or a re-clone to rejoin.
What it checks:

the old primary comes back as a replica with no manual step;
the 5 divergent rows are gone from every node;
the logs say which method was used.
The test accepts either method, as the brief does, but fails if the logs show neither.

1. Write the test
tee tests/fo-rejoin.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# FO-5: after real divergence, the old primary rejoins automatically as a replica.
# Divergence: rows committed only on the old primary (replication cut, synchronous_commit=local),
# then its node is powered off so a replica is promoted without them.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

node=""; old_ip=""
peers=(); peer_pods=()
cut_done=""

psql_pod() { timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c "$2"; }

cut() {
  local ip
  for ip in "${peers[@]}"; do
    docker exec "$node" iptables -w -I FORWARD 1 -s "$old_ip" -d "$ip" -j DROP
    docker exec "$node" iptables -w -I FORWARD 1 -s "$ip" -d "$old_ip" -j DROP
  done
  cut_done=yes
}
heal() {
  local ip
  for ip in "${peers[@]}"; do
    docker exec "$node" iptables -w -D FORWARD -s "$old_ip" -d "$ip" -j DROP 2>/dev/null || true
    docker exec "$node" iptables -w -D FORWARD -s "$ip" -d "$old_ip" -j DROP 2>/dev/null || true
  done
  cut_done=""
}
cleanup() {
  if [ -n "$node" ]; then
    if [ "$(docker inspect -f '{{.State.Running}}' "$node" 2>/dev/null || echo false)" != "true" ]; then
      echo "cleanup: starting $node again"
      docker start "$node" >/dev/null || true
    elif [ -n "$cut_done" ]; then
      echo "cleanup: removing the replication cut"
      heal
    fi
  fi
}
trap cleanup EXIT

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"
old=$(current_leader)
node=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$old" -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
old_ip=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$old" -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
tl_before=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')
pods_json=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pods -l application=pg-ha,cluster-name=pg-ha -o json)
mapfile -t peers < <(jq -r --arg o "$old" '.items[] | select(.metadata.name != $o) | .status.podIP' <<<"$pods_json")
mapfile -t peer_pods < <(jq -r --arg o "$old" '.items[] | select(.metadata.name != $o) | .metadata.name' <<<"$pods_json")
echo "leader before: $old (timeline $tl_before) on $node, replicas: ${peer_pods[*]}"

psql_pod "$old" "drop table if exists rejoin_probe" >/dev/null 2>&1
psql_pod "$old" "create table rejoin_probe(id bigserial primary key, note text not null)" >/dev/null
psql_pod "$old" "insert into rejoin_probe(note) values ('baseline')" >/dev/null
for p in "${peer_pods[@]}"; do
  ok=""
  for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
    c=$(psql_pod "$p" "select count(*) from rejoin_probe where note = 'baseline'" 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "$c" = 1 ]; then ok=yes; break; fi
    sleep 1
  done
  [ "$ok" = yes ] || fail "the baseline row did not reach $p"
done
pass "baseline row replicated to both replicas"

echo "cutting replication between $old and its replicas (API access stays up)"
cut
timeout 25 kubectl -n "$NS" exec -i "$old" -c postgres -- \
  psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 >/dev/null <<'SQL'
set synchronous_commit = local;
insert into rejoin_probe(note) select 'divergent-' || g from generate_series(1,5) g;
SQL
n_old=$(psql_pod "$old" "select count(*) from rejoin_probe where note like 'divergent-%'")
[ "$n_old" = 5 ] || fail "expected 5 divergent rows on the old primary, found $n_old"
sleep 5
for p in "${peer_pods[@]}"; do
  c=$(psql_pod "$p" "select count(*) from rejoin_probe where note like 'divergent-%'")
  [ "$c" = 0 ] || fail "the cut did not work: $p already has $c divergent row(s)"
done
pass "5 rows exist only on the old primary $old (replicas have none)"

echo "powering off node $node (docker kill)"
docker kill "$node" >/dev/null
new=""
deadline=$((SECONDS + 180))
while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
  new=$(current_leader 2>/dev/null || true)
  if [ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$old" ]; then break; fi
  sleep 3
done
echo "leader after: $new"
[ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$old" ] || fail "no replica was promoted"
pass "replica $new was promoted (it never had the divergent rows)"

echo "starting node $node again"
docker start "$node" >/dev/null
cut_done=""
wait_healthy 600 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table
role=$(cluster_json | jq -r --arg m "$old" '.[] | select(.Member == $m) | .Role')
echo "old primary $old now has role: $role"
case "$role" in
  Replica|"Sync Standby") pass "old primary $old rejoined as a replica with no manual step" ;;
  *) fail "old primary $old has unexpected role '$role'" ;;
esac

for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
  d=$(psql_pod "$p" "select count(*) from rejoin_probe where note like 'divergent-%'")
  b=$(psql_pod "$p" "select count(*) from rejoin_probe where note = 'baseline'")
  echo "$p: divergent rows=$d, baseline rows=$b"
  [ "$d" = 0 ] || fail "$p still has divergent rows"
  [ "$b" = 1 ] || fail "$p lost the baseline row"
done
pass "divergent rows are gone from every node, the baseline row is on every node"

logs=$( { kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$old" -c postgres 2>/dev/null; kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$old" -c postgres --previous 2>/dev/null; } || true)
echo "--- rejoin-related log lines from $old ---"
grep -iE "pg_rewind|rewind|reinitializ|bootstrap from|basebackup" <<<"$logs" | tail -12 || true
if grep -qi "running pg_rewind" <<<"$logs"; then
  method="pg_rewind"
elif grep -qiE "reinitializ|bootstrap from|basebackup" <<<"$logs"; then
  method="re-clone"
else
  method="unknown"
fi
echo "rejoin method: $method"
[ "$method" != unknown ] || fail "the logs do not show how $old rejoined"
pass "rejoin method identified from the logs: $method"

tl_after=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')
echo "timeline before: $tl_before, after: $tl_after"
[ "$tl_after" -gt "$tl_before" ] || fail "timeline did not advance"
pass "timeline advanced ($tl_before -> $tl_after)"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/fo-rejoin.sh
shellcheck tests/fo-rejoin.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"

It’s read-only apart from two temporary rules on the primary’s node, which it removes at the end. It tries to open a new connection from the primary to a replica with the rules in place, and prints the packet counters, so we can see whether the rules matched any traffic:

docker exec $node iptables -w -D FORWARD -s $peer_ip -d $old_ip -j DROP

How I’ll read it
CONNECTED and the first two counters are 0: the rules never matched the traffic, so pod-to-pod packets don’t pass through that chain. I’ll move the rules to a table that sees the packets before routing.
blocked, but pg_stat_replication still shows streaming with replay_lsn advancing: new connections are blocked, but the established replication connection survives. That’s the same effect as in FO-4, and the fix is again to drop established traffic.
blocked and the replication state freezes: the cut works, and the failure came from timing in my test. I’d then wait for the lag to appear before writing the rows.

hmsmiraz@HMSMIRAZ:~/postgres-ha-lab$ bash -s <<'EOF' set -u NS=pg-ha old=$(kubectl -n $NS get pods -l application=pg-ha,role=primary -o jsonpath='{.items[0].metadata.name}') node=$(kubectl -n $NS get pod $old -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}') old_ip=$(kubectl -n $NS get pod $old -o jsonpath='{.st

PASTED
The observation shows what went wrong, and it’s a different problem from the one I guessed.

The cut worked for about 4 s, then stopped working. At t+3 s both replicas had 0 divergent rows and replay was behind sent, so the rules were dropping the WAL. By t+6 s both had all 5 rows.
The rule order changed. In the last diagnostic our DROP rules sat at lines 1 and 2, with cali-FORWARD at line 3. In this run cali-FORWARD is at line 1 and our four DROP rules are at lines 2 to 5. Calico’s agent (Felix) re-inserted its own jump at the top of the chain, so traffic now reaches Calico’s accept rules before our drops. The counters match: our rules only caught the first 5 to 6 packets, while cali-FORWARD saw 77.
I’m confident in what happened, but it’s still my reading of the counters and not a proven cause. The next run tests it.

FO-4 is not affected. There, a replica could only be promoted after the leader lock stopped renewing, and the renewTime check held for the full window. That’s independent evidence that the cut was real in that test.

The fix is a rule Calico doesn’t manage. Calico owns the iptables chains, so it can reorder anything there. A separate nftables table with an earlier hook priority (-150, before Calico’s filter hook at 0) is outside its control. The check below first confirms that nft exists on the node image. I haven’t verified that it does.

done
echo "--- nft counters ---"

hmsmiraz@HMSMIRAZ:~/postgres-ha-lab$ bash -s <<'EOF' set -u NS=pg-ha old=$(kubectl -n $NS get pods -l application=pg-ha,role=primary -o jsonpath='{.items[0].metadata.name}') node=$(kubectl -n $NS get pod $old -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}') old_ip=$(kubectl -n $NS get pod $old -o jsonpath='{.st

PASTED
This run proves nothing about the approach, because the cut was never installed. All six nft commands failed with syntax errors, and table ip pgha_fence {} printed with no chain and no rules. Of course the rows reached the replicas, since nothing was blocking them. The cleanup worked (No such file or directory at the end), so nothing is left on the node.

The cause, with one caveat. The error points at the word fwd, my chain name. In nftables, fwd is a reserved keyword (the packet-forwarding statement), so it can’t be used as a name. I’m fairly sure that’s the whole problem, but I haven’t proven it. If the next run still fails, the first lines will say so. The fix is a different chain name, isolate, and the script now stops with a clear message if any nft command fails, so it can’t run a “cut” that doesn’t exist.

1. Run it again
bash -s <<'EOF'
set -u
NS=pg-ha
old=$(kubectl -n $NS get pods -l application=pg-ha,role=primary -o jsonpath='{.items[0].metadata.name}')
node=$(kubectl -n $NS get pod $old -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
old_ip=$(kubectl -n $NS get pod $old -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
ips=()
for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
  [ "$p" = "$old" ] && continue
  ips+=("$(kubectl -n $NS get pod $p -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')")
done
q() { kubectl -n $NS exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -c "$2"; }
add() {
  docker exec $node nft add table ip pgha_fence || return 1
  docker exec $node nft "add chain ip pgha_fence isolate { type filter hook forward priority -150 ; policy accept ; }" || return 1
  for ip in "${ips[@]}"; do
    docker exec $node nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr $old_ip ip daddr $ip counter drop || return 1
    docker exec $node nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr $ip ip daddr $old_ip counter drop || return 1
  done
}
del() { docker exec $node nft delete table ip pgha_fence 2>/dev/null; }

echo "primary $old on $node ($old_ip); replicas: ${ips[*]}"
q $old "drop table if exists diag_probe"

done

docker exec $node nft list table ip pgha_fence

1. Save a short note on the method
The raw terminal output is in the session log, so this note records what was observed:

tee docs/evidence/FO-5-method-notes.md > /dev/null <<'EOF'
# FO-5: how the replication cut was built (observations from 2026-11-19)

Goal: commit rows on the primary that never reach any replica, then lose the primary.

1. First attempt, `iptables -I FORWARD 1 ... -j DROP` on the primary's node: the cut held for about
   4 s, then the rows reached both replicas. The rule counters showed our rules had caught only the
   first packets, while `cali-FORWARD` had moved to line 1 of the chain. Reading: Calico's agent
   re-inserted its jump ahead of our rules. This is an interpretation of the counters, not a proven
   cause. The test stopped on its own safety check (`the cut did not work`). Run saved as
   FO-5-run1-FAILED.txt.
2. Second attempt, a separate nftables table (`ip pgha_fence`, chain hook `forward`, priority -150,
   which `nft` prints as `mangle`): both replicas stayed at 0 divergent rows for 30 s while the
   primary had 5; `replay` on the replicas stayed frozen. Each rule matched 9 packets. The table was
   removed afterwards.
3. A first nft attempt failed on a syntax error because `fwd` is a reserved word in nftables; the
   chain is named `isolate`.

The test refuses to continue if the replicas already hold the rows, so a rejoin can never "pass"
without a real divergence.
EOF
2. Replace the FO-5 test
It now uses the nft table. It checks the cut twice, 5 s and 15 s after the write, and stops if any nft command fails:

tee tests/fo-rejoin.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# FO-5: after real divergence, the old primary rejoins automatically as a replica.
# Divergence: rows committed only on the old primary (replication cut, synchronous_commit=local),
# then its node is powered off so a replica is promoted without them.
# The cut is a separate nftables table at priority -150, so Calico's own chains cannot reorder it.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

node=""; old_ip=""
peers=(); peer_pods=()
cut_done=""

psql_pod() { timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c "$2"; }

cut() {
  local ip
  docker exec "$node" nft add table ip pgha_fence || return 1
  docker exec "$node" nft "add chain ip pgha_fence isolate { type filter hook forward priority -150 ; policy accept ; }" || return 1
  for ip in "${peers[@]}"; do
    docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$old_ip" ip daddr "$ip" counter drop || return 1
    docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$ip" ip daddr "$old_ip" counter drop || return 1
  done
  cut_done=yes
}
heal() {
  docker exec "$node" nft delete table ip pgha_fence 2>/dev/null || true
  cut_done=""
}
cleanup() {
  if [ -n "$node" ]; then
    if [ "$(docker inspect -f '{{.State.Running}}' "$node" 2>/dev/null || echo false)" != "true" ]; then
      echo "cleanup: starting $node again"
      docker start "$node" >/dev/null || true
    elif [ -n "$cut_done" ]; then
      echo "cleanup: removing the replication cut"
      heal
    fi
  fi
}
trap cleanup EXIT

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"
old=$(current_leader)
node=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$old" -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
old_ip=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$old" -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
tl_before=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')
pods_json=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pods -l application=pg-ha,cluster-name=pg-ha -o json)
mapfile -t peers < <(jq -r --arg o "$old" '.items[] | select(.metadata.name != $o) | .status.podIP' <<<"$pods_json")
mapfile -t peer_pods < <(jq -r --arg o "$old" '.items[] | select(.metadata.name != $o) | .metadata.name' <<<"$pods_json")
echo "leader before: $old (timeline $tl_before) on $node, replicas: ${peer_pods[*]}"

psql_pod "$old" "drop table if exists rejoin_probe" >/dev/null 2>&1
psql_pod "$old" "create table rejoin_probe(id bigserial primary key, note text not null)" >/dev/null
psql_pod "$old" "insert into rejoin_probe(note) values ('baseline')" >/dev/null
for p in "${peer_pods[@]}"; do
  ok=""
  for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
    c=$(psql_pod "$p" "select count(*) from rejoin_probe where note = 'baseline'" 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "$c" = 1 ]; then ok=yes; break; fi
    sleep 1
  done
  [ "$ok" = yes ] || fail "the baseline row did not reach $p"
done
pass "baseline row replicated to both replicas"

echo "cutting replication between $old and its replicas (nftables, API access stays up)"
cut || fail "the nft rules could not be installed, so there is no cut to test"
docker exec "$node" nft list table ip pgha_fence
timeout 25 kubectl -n "$NS" exec -i "$old" -c postgres -- \
  psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 >/dev/null <<'SQL'
set synchronous_commit = local;
insert into rejoin_probe(note) select 'divergent-' || g from generate_series(1,5) g;
SQL
n_old=$(psql_pod "$old" "select count(*) from rejoin_probe where note like 'divergent-%'")
[ "$n_old" = 5 ] || fail "expected 5 divergent rows on the old primary, found $n_old"
for wait_s in 5 10; do
  sleep "$wait_s"
  for p in "${peer_pods[@]}"; do
    c=$(psql_pod "$p" "select count(*) from rejoin_probe where note like 'divergent-%'")
    [ "$c" = 0 ] || fail "the cut did not hold: $p has $c divergent row(s)"
  done
done
pass "5 rows exist only on the old primary $old (replicas still have none after 15 s)"

echo "powering off node $node (docker kill)"
docker kill "$node" >/dev/null
cut_done=""
new=""
deadline=$((SECONDS + 180))
while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
  new=$(current_leader 2>/dev/null || true)
  if [ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$old" ]; then break; fi
  sleep 3
done
echo "leader after: $new"
[ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$old" ] || fail "no replica was promoted"
pass "replica $new was promoted (it never had the divergent rows)"

echo "starting node $node again"
docker start "$node" >/dev/null
wait_healthy 600 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table
role=$(cluster_json | jq -r --arg m "$old" '.[] | select(.Member == $m) | .Role')
echo "old primary $old now has role: $role"
case "$role" in
  Replica|"Sync Standby") pass "old primary $old rejoined as a replica with no manual step" ;;
  *) fail "old primary $old has unexpected role '$role'" ;;
esac

for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
  d=$(psql_pod "$p" "select count(*) from rejoin_probe where note like 'divergent-%'")
  b=$(psql_pod "$p" "select count(*) from rejoin_probe where note = 'baseline'")
  echo "$p: divergent rows=$d, baseline rows=$b"
  [ "$d" = 0 ] || fail "$p still has divergent rows"
  [ "$b" = 1 ] || fail "$p lost the baseline row"
done
pass "divergent rows are gone from every node, the baseline row is on every node"

logs=$( { kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$old" -c postgres 2>/dev/null; kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$old" -c postgres --previous 2>/dev/null; } || true)
echo "--- rejoin-related log lines from $old ---"
grep -iE "pg_rewind|rewind|reinitializ|bootstrap from|basebackup" <<<"$logs" | tail -12 || true
if grep -qi "running pg_rewind" <<<"$logs"; then
  method="pg_rewind"
elif grep -qiE "reinitializ|bootstrap from|basebackup" <<<"$logs"; then
  method="re-clone"
else
  method="unknown"
fi
echo "rejoin method: $method"
[ "$method" != unknown ] || fail "the logs do not show how $old rejoined"
pass "rejoin method identified from the logs: $method"

tl_after=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')
echo "timeline before: $tl_before, after: $tl_after"
[ "$tl_after" -gt "$tl_before" ] || fail "timeline did not advance"
pass "timeline advanced ($tl_before -> $tl_after)"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
shellcheck tests/fo-rejoin.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"

Steps left in Phase 2: three
REP-3 drill (next). A stopped replica must not fill the primary’s disk.
ADR-0001 degraded case. What writes do when no sync standby is available.
FT-1 and FT-2 check. Spread across nodes and zones, and the PodDisruptionBudget blocking a node drain.
Phase 2, step 9: REP-3 drill, a lagging slot can’t fill the disk
What the drill does:

It lowers max_slot_wal_keep_size from 1 GB to 128 MB through Patroni’s configuration, the supported way. It’s a test-only change, and the test puts it back at the end.
It cuts one async replica off with the same nft method (the sync standby stays connected, so writes keep working).
It forces 24 WAL segments (about 384 MB), three times the cap, then runs CHECKPOINT.
It checks that the cut replica’s slot was invalidated or dropped, and that the primary’s pg_wal shrank back near the cap instead of holding all of it.
It removes the cut and records whether the replica recovers by itself.
What I expect, but haven’t seen: the replica probably can’t catch up on its own. The WAL it needs has been deleted, and we have no WAL archive yet. If so, the test runs patronictl reinit and says so plainly. That finding goes in the write-up, and the archive in Phase 3 is the real fix. If it does recover, I’ll be wrong, and the output will show it.

Disk: the generated WAL peaks around 400 MB per node and is released afterwards. The nodes have plenty of room.

1. Write the test
tee tests/rep-slot-disk.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# REP-3 drill: a replica that stops consuming WAL must not be able to fill the primary's disk.
# max_slot_wal_keep_size is lowered to 128MB for the drill (via Patroni) and restored afterwards.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

CAP=128MB
ORIG=1GB
ROUNDS=24
node=""; cut_done=""; cap_changed=""

ctl() { timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml "$@"; }
psql_pod() { timeout 30 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c "$2"; }
wal_mb() { timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader" -c postgres -- sh -c 'du -sm /pgdata/data/pg_wal | cut -f1'; }
slot_row() { psql_pod "$leader" "select coalesce(wal_status,'-')||'|'||coalesce(invalidation_reason,'-')||'|'||active from pg_replication_slots where slot_name = '$slot'"; }

cleanup() {
  if [ -n "$cut_done" ]; then docker exec "$node" nft delete table ip pgha_fence 2>/dev/null || true; fi
  if [ -n "$cap_changed" ]; then
    echo "cleanup: restoring max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG"
    ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG" --force >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  fi
}
trap cleanup EXIT

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"
leader=$(current_leader)
target=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Replica") | .Member' | head -1)
[ -n "$target" ] || fail "no async replica to use for the drill"
slot=${target//-/_}
node=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$leader" -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
leader_ip=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$leader" -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
target_ip=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$target" -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
echo "leader: $leader on $node ($leader_ip); drill replica: $target ($target_ip), slot $slot"
echo "slot before: $(slot_row)"
echo "pg_wal size before: $(wal_mb) MB"

ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$CAP" --force >/dev/null
cap_changed=yes
v=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  v=$(psql_pod "$leader" "show max_slot_wal_keep_size" || true)
  [ "$v" = "$CAP" ] && break
  sleep 2
done
[ "$v" = "$CAP" ] || fail "max_slot_wal_keep_size is '$v', expected $CAP"
pass "max_slot_wal_keep_size lowered to $CAP for the drill"

psql_pod "$leader" "create table if not exists slot_probe(i int)" >/dev/null

echo "cutting $target off from the primary (nftables)"
docker exec "$node" nft add table ip pgha_fence
docker exec "$node" nft "add chain ip pgha_fence isolate { type filter hook forward priority -150 ; policy accept ; }"
docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$leader_ip" ip daddr "$target_ip" counter drop
docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$target_ip" ip daddr "$leader_ip" counter drop
cut_done=yes

echo "generating $ROUNDS WAL segments (about $((ROUNDS * 16)) MB) while $target is cut off"
for i in $(seq 1 "$ROUNDS"); do echo "insert into slot_probe values ($i); select pg_switch_wal();"; done \
  | timeout 180 kubectl -n "$NS" exec -i "$leader" -c postgres -- \
      psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 >/dev/null \
  || fail "the primary could not keep writing while the replica was cut off"
pass "the primary kept accepting writes while $target was cut off"

echo "slot before checkpoint: $(slot_row)"
size_before=$(wal_mb)
echo "pg_wal size before checkpoint: ${size_before} MB"
psql_pod "$leader" "checkpoint" >/dev/null

seen=""
for _ in $(seq 1 60); do
  row=$(slot_row || true)
  case "$row" in
    lost*|*"|wal_removed|"*) seen="slot invalidated ($row)"; break ;;
    "") seen="slot was dropped (Patroni removes slots whose WAL is lost)"; break ;;
  esac
  sleep 1
done
echo "slot after checkpoint: ${row:-<none>}"
[ -n "$seen" ] || fail "the lagging slot was never invalidated; WAL retention is not capped"
pass "lagging slot was handled by the cap: $seen"

psql_pod "$leader" "checkpoint" >/dev/null
sleep 3
size_after=$(wal_mb)
echo "pg_wal size after checkpoint: ${size_after} MB (was ${size_before} MB)"
[ "$size_after" -le 256 ] || fail "pg_wal is ${size_after} MB, above 256 MB (cap $CAP plus headroom)"
pass "pg_wal stayed bounded: ${size_after} MB (cap $CAP)"

echo "removing the cut"
docker exec "$node" nft delete table ip pgha_fence
cut_done=""
recovered=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  st=$(cluster_json | jq -r --arg m "$target" '.[] | select(.Member == $m) | .State')
  if [ "$st" = streaming ]; then recovered=yes; break; fi
  sleep 4
done
if [ -n "$recovered" ]; then
  echo "OBSERVATION: $target caught up and is streaming again on its own"
else
  echo "OBSERVATION: $target did NOT recover on its own (state: ${st:-unknown}); the WAL it needs was removed and there is no WAL archive yet"
  echo "running: patronictl reinit pg-ha $target (manual step, recorded as a finding)"
  ctl reinit pg-ha "$target" --force >/dev/null
fi
wait_healthy 400 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table
pass "cluster is back to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"

ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG" --force >/dev/null
cap_changed=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  v=$(psql_pod "$leader" "show max_slot_wal_keep_size" || true)
  [ "$v" = "$ORIG" ] && break
  sleep 2
done
[ "$v" = "$ORIG" ] || fail "max_slot_wal_keep_size was not restored (it is '$v')"
pass "max_slot_wal_keep_size restored to $ORIG"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/rep-slot-disk.sh
shellcheck tests/rep-slot-disk.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"

What the partial output already shows
The cap took effect. The slot went from reserved to unreserved once the WAL kept for it passed 128 MB.

433 MB is expected at this point. Postgres only removes old WAL at a checkpoint, and the test hadn’t run one yet.
Why it stopped (a guess)
The next command was CHECKPOINT, and my helper gives every command a 30 s limit. Flushing about 433 MB on WSL’s disk could easily take longer. With set -e, a timeout exits the script without a message, which is a flaw in my test. I haven’t confirmed this. The fix below also makes any failure print its line number, so a different cause will name itself.

The replica didn’t heal itself, and the log points at a second problem that I described wrongly earlier.

What the output shows
pg-ha-2 is stuck. Its lag stayed at exactly 382 MB and 368 MB for all 12 readings over two minutes. Its slot pg_ha_2 is active = f, so nothing is streaming to it.
My earlier prediction was wrong. I expected the WAL it needs to be gone. The slot says reserved and the leader’s pg_wal still holds 449 MB, so the WAL is still there. Something else keeps pg-ha-2 from connecting.
The log gives a concrete error, and it’s my config’s fault. It says no pg_hba.conf entry for replication connection from host "127.0.0.1", user "replicator". My pg_hba allows replication only from 192.168.0.0/16, so Patroni can’t open its local replication connection to read the replica’s timeline. That explains the blank TL column. I called the same Can not fetch local timeline errors in FO-4 “transient”. That was wrong, since the missing rule makes them permanent noise, and I’ll correct it in the write-up.
What I don’t know is whether that missing rule is why pg-ha-2 isn’t streaming. Earlier replicas streamed fine with the same error in the log, so it may only be noise. The checks below are read-only, so we find the real cause before changing anything.

1. The replica’s own view
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-2 -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atc "select pg_is_in_recovery(), pg_last_wal_receive_lsn(), pg_last_wal_replay_lsn()"
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-2 -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atc "select status, receive_start_lsn, flushed_lsn, sender_host, slot_name from pg_stat_wal_receiver"
kubectl -n pg-ha get pod pg-ha-2 --show-labels
An empty result for the second query would mean the replica has no WAL receiver running at all.

2. The Postgres lines in both logs
The first command filters out the Python traceback lines so the real messages stand out:

kubectl -n pg-ha logs pg-ha-2 -c postgres --since=3h | grep -vE '^\s|Traceback|^psycopg2|^connection to server' | grep -iE "FATAL|ERROR|LOG:|WARNING|walreceiver|streaming|timeline|reinit|following|restart" | tail -30
kubectl -n pg-ha logs pg-ha-0 -c postgres --since=3h | grep -iE "walsender|pg_ha_2|terminat|timeout|replication" | tail -15
How I’ll read the results
The WAL receiver is missing or can’t connect, with an authentication or pg_hba error in the log: the pod’s replication setup is broken. I’ll fix pg_hba and the check together.
The WAL receiver exists but sits in waiting or stopping, with timeout lines: the old connection never recovered after the nft cut. That would be a real finding about partitions on a running replica, and I’d check the walsender timeout settings.
No Postgres error at all: Patroni itself is stuck, and I’d look at its loop in the logs.

hmsmiraz@HMSMIRAZ:~/postgres-ha-lab$ kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-2 -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atc "select pg_is_in_recovery(), pg_last_wal_receive_lsn(), pg_last_wal_replay_lsn()" kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-2 -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atc "select status, receive_start_lsn, flushed_lsn

PASTED
The log gives the real cause, and it confirms my first prediction, which I then wrongly talked myself out of.

The replica can’t recover because the WAL it needs is gone. The log says requested WAL segment 000000070000000000000008 has already been removed, repeating every 5 s. pg-ha-2 is stuck at 0/8000000 and asks for that segment, but the primary no longer has it.
Why the slot looked healthy. It says reserved, yet the segment is gone. My explanation, which I haven’t confirmed: the drill’s checkpoint probably did run on the server even though my client timed out. With the 128 MB cap, WAL the slot needed was removed and the slot became lost. Patroni 4.1.4’s release notes say it drops lost slots and recreates them, so the new slot starts from the current position and says reserved. That fits everything on screen.
So the cap does bound the disk, and the cost is that a replica that falls too far behind can’t catch up by itself. Nothing in the cluster can fix that without a WAL archive. That’s a real limitation until Phase 3, and it goes into known-limitations.md.
The pg_hba error is a separate bug of mine. The rule for replicator allows only 192.168.0.0/16, but Patroni checks its own timeline over 127.0.0.1. That causes the Can not fetch local timeline errors every 5 s on every replica. I called the FO-4 instances of it “transient”, and that was wrong.
1. Heal pg-ha-2
This is the manual step the cluster currently needs. We record it as a finding, not as a normal operation:

kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml reinit pg-ha pg-ha-2 --force
for i in $(seq 1 20); do
  kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list | grep -E "pg-ha-2"
  sleep 6
done
You should see pg-ha-2 go through creating replica or running and end up streaming with lag 0.

2. Fix the pg_hba rule
sed -i '/- host all all 127.0.0.1\/32 scram-sha-256/a\        - host replication replicator 127.0.0.1/32 scram-sha-256' k8s/pg/20-config.yaml
grep -n "replicator" k8s/pg/20-config.yaml
kubectl apply -f k8s/pg/20-config.yaml
The grep should show two replicator lines: the original one and the new 127.0.0.1/32 one.

The mounted file takes up to about a minute to update. Wait for it, then tell Patroni to reload (nothing restarts):

for i in $(seq 1 20); do
  n=$(kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-1 -c postgres -- grep -c "replication replicator 127.0.0.1/32" /etc/patroni/patroni.yml)
  [ "$n" = 1 ] && break
  sleep 6
done
echo "new rule visible in the pod: $n"
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml reload pg-ha --force
sleep 20
for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do echo "$p: $(kubectl -n pg-ha logs $p -c postgres --since=30s | grep -c 'no pg_hba.conf entry for replication connection from host "127.0.0.1"') errors in the last 30 s"; done
All three counts should be 0. If they aren’t, don’t force anything. Send me the output, because I’m not certain that a reload rewrites pg_hba in this setup.

3. Replace the drill test
I changed the pass criteria after looking at the real behaviour:

The size check was wrong. Postgres recycles old segments, so pg_wal can stay large after the checkpoint even though the WAL is no longer retained. That’s why it showed 449 MB while the segment was gone. The test now prints the size and doesn’t assert on it.
A better proof. The test records the WAL file the replica needs before the cut, then checks that this file no longer exists on the primary after the checkpoint. That is the cap doing its job.
Failures are no longer silent. Any failure prints its line number, and the checkpoints get a 180 s limit.
tee tests/rep-slot-disk.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# REP-3 drill: a replica that stops consuming WAL must not be able to make the primary keep WAL forever.
# max_slot_wal_keep_size is lowered to 128MB for the drill (via Patroni) and restored afterwards.
# Evidence: the WAL file the replica still needs is removed from the primary, while writes continue.
set -Eeuo pipefail
trap 'echo "FAIL: command failed at line $LINENO: $BASH_COMMAND"' ERR
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

CAP=128MB
ORIG=1GB
ROUNDS=24
node=""; cut_done=""; cap_changed=""

ctl() { timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml "$@"; }
psql_pod() { timeout 30 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c "$2"; }
checkpoint() { timeout 180 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atq -c checkpoint >/dev/null; }
wal_mb() { timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader" -c postgres -- sh -c 'du -sm /pgdata/data/pg_wal | cut -f1'; }
slot_row() { psql_pod "$leader" "select coalesce(wal_status,'-')||'|'||coalesce(invalidation_reason,'-')||'|'||active||'|safe_wal_size='||coalesce(safe_wal_size::text,'-') from pg_replication_slots where slot_name = '$slot'"; }

cleanup() {
  if [ -n "$cut_done" ]; then docker exec "$node" nft delete table ip pgha_fence 2>/dev/null || true; fi
  if [ -n "$cap_changed" ]; then
    echo "cleanup: restoring max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG"
    ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG" --force >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  fi
}
trap cleanup EXIT

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"
leader=$(current_leader)
target=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Replica") | .Member' | head -1)
[ -n "$target" ] || fail "no async replica to use for the drill"
slot=${target//-/_}
node=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$leader" -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
leader_ip=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$leader" -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
target_ip=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$target" -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
need_lsn=$(psql_pod "$target" "select pg_last_wal_replay_lsn()")
need_file=$(psql_pod "$leader" "select pg_walfile_name('$need_lsn')")
echo "leader: $leader on $node ($leader_ip); drill replica: $target ($target_ip), slot $slot"
echo "replica replay position: $need_lsn, WAL file it needs: $need_file"
echo "slot before: $(slot_row)"
echo "pg_wal size before: $(wal_mb) MB"

ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$CAP" --force >/dev/null
cap_changed=yes
v=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  v=$(psql_pod "$leader" "show max_slot_wal_keep_size" || true)
  [ "$v" = "$CAP" ] && break
  sleep 2
done
[ "$v" = "$CAP" ] || fail "max_slot_wal_keep_size is '$v', expected $CAP"
pass "max_slot_wal_keep_size lowered to $CAP for the drill"

psql_pod "$leader" "create table if not exists slot_probe(i int)" >/dev/null

echo "cutting $target off from the primary (nftables)"
docker exec "$node" nft add table ip pgha_fence
docker exec "$node" nft "add chain ip pgha_fence isolate { type filter hook forward priority -150 ; policy accept ; }"
docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$leader_ip" ip daddr "$target_ip" counter drop
docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$target_ip" ip daddr "$leader_ip" counter drop
cut_done=yes

echo "generating $ROUNDS WAL segments (about $((ROUNDS * 16)) MB) while $target is cut off"
for i in $(seq 1 "$ROUNDS"); do echo "insert into slot_probe values ($i); select pg_switch_wal();"; done \
  | timeout 180 kubectl -n "$NS" exec -i "$leader" -c postgres -- \
      psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 >/dev/null \
  || fail "the primary could not keep writing while the replica was cut off"
pass "the primary kept accepting writes while $target was cut off"

before_row=$(slot_row)
echo "slot before checkpoint: $before_row"
echo "pg_wal size before checkpoint: $(wal_mb) MB"
case "$before_row" in
  unreserved*|lost*) pass "the slot is past the cap (${before_row%%|*})" ;;
  *) fail "the slot is still within the cap: $before_row" ;;
esac

checkpoint
checkpoint
gone=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  c=$(psql_pod "$leader" "select count(*) from pg_ls_waldir() where name = '$need_file'")
  if [ "$c" = 0 ]; then gone=yes; break; fi
  sleep 2
done
echo "slot after checkpoints: $(slot_row || true)"
[ -n "$gone" ] || fail "the WAL file $need_file the lagging replica needs is still on the primary; retention is not capped"
pass "the WAL file $need_file needed by $target was removed from the primary (retention is capped)"
echo "pg_wal size after checkpoints: $(wal_mb) MB (informational: Postgres keeps recycled segments, so this is not the retention measure)"

echo "removing the cut"
docker exec "$node" nft delete table ip pgha_fence
cut_done=""
recovered=""
st=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  st=$(cluster_json | jq -r --arg m "$target" '.[] | select(.Member == $m) | .State')
  if [ "$st" = streaming ]; then recovered=yes; break; fi
  sleep 4
done
if [ -n "$recovered" ]; then
  echo "OBSERVATION: $target caught up and is streaming again on its own"
else
  echo "OBSERVATION: $target did NOT recover on its own (state: ${st:-unknown}); the WAL it needs was removed and there is no WAL archive yet"
  echo "running: patronictl reinit pg-ha $target (manual step, recorded as a finding)"
  ctl reinit pg-ha "$target" --force >/dev/null
fi
wait_healthy 400 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table
pass "cluster is back to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"

ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG" --force >/dev/null
cap_changed=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  v=$(psql_pod "$leader" "show max_slot_wal_keep_size" || true)
  [ "$v" = "$ORIG" ] && break
  sleep 2
done
[ "$v" = "$ORIG" ] || fail "max_slot_wal_keep_size was not restored (it is '$v')"
pass "max_slot_wal_keep_size restored to $ORIG"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
shellcheck tests/rep-slot-disk.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"
4. Run it
Only run it after step 1 shows pg-ha-2 as streaming with lag 0. Don’t press anything while it runs (about 4 to 8 minutes). The test removes its nft table and restores the 1 GB setting itself, even if it fails:

bash tests/rep-slot-disk.sh 2>&1 | tee docs/evidence/REP-3-drill.txt

What worked
pg-ha-2 is healed. After reinit it went to streaming within about 12 s, and the lag is 0. That is the manual step I said the cluster needs after a replica loses its WAL, and it’s now recorded as a finding.

What did not work

The reload only applies if Patroni rewrites pg_hba.conf, and I assumed it did. It evidently hasn’t done that here, or hasn’t done it yet. This is a failed assumption of mine.
My reload was the wrong tool. patronictl reload re-reads Patroni’s own file, but pg_hba rules written under bootstrap.dcs are stored in the DCS after the first start. Once the cluster exists, a change to this file doesn’t reach them. They have to be changed through patronictl edit-config, and that’s the part I missed.
A trap for the next person. Because the rule also has to be in the file for new clusters, I need both places: the ConfigMap (fresh installs, already done) and the live DCS config (this running cluster).

1. Install the reworked drill test
tee tests/rep-slot-disk.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# REP-3 drill: a replica that stops consuming WAL must not be able to make the primary keep WAL forever.
# max_slot_wal_keep_size is lowered to 128MB for the drill (via Patroni) and restored afterwards.
# Evidence: the WAL file the replica still needs is removed from the primary, while writes continue.
set -Eeuo pipefail
trap 'echo "FAIL: command failed at line $LINENO: $BASH_COMMAND"' ERR
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

CAP=128MB
ORIG=1GB
ROUNDS=24
node=""; cut_done=""; cap_changed=""

ctl() { timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml "$@"; }
psql_pod() { timeout 30 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c "$2"; }
checkpoint() { timeout 180 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atq -c checkpoint >/dev/null; }
wal_mb() { timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader" -c postgres -- sh -c 'du -sm /pgdata/data/pg_wal | cut -f1'; }
slot_row() { psql_pod "$leader" "select coalesce(wal_status,'-')||'|'||coalesce(invalidation_reason,'-')||'|'||active||'|safe_wal_size='||coalesce(safe_wal_size::text,'-') from pg_replication_slots where slot_name = '$slot'"; }

cleanup() {
  if [ -n "$cut_done" ]; then docker exec "$node" nft delete table ip pgha_fence 2>/dev/null || true; fi
  if [ -n "$cap_changed" ]; then
    echo "cleanup: restoring max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG"
    ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG" --force >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  fi
}
trap cleanup EXIT

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"
leader=$(current_leader)
target=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Replica") | .Member' | head -1)
[ -n "$target" ] || fail "no async replica to use for the drill"
slot=${target//-/_}
node=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$leader" -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
leader_ip=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$leader" -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
target_ip=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$target" -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
need_lsn=$(psql_pod "$target" "select pg_last_wal_replay_lsn()")
need_file=$(psql_pod "$leader" "select pg_walfile_name('$need_lsn')")
echo "leader: $leader on $node ($leader_ip); drill replica: $target ($target_ip), slot $slot"
echo "replica replay position: $need_lsn, WAL file it needs: $need_file"
echo "slot before: $(slot_row)"
echo "pg_wal size before: $(wal_mb) MB"

ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$CAP" --force >/dev/null
cap_changed=yes
v=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  v=$(psql_pod "$leader" "show max_slot_wal_keep_size" || true)
  [ "$v" = "$CAP" ] && break
  sleep 2
done
[ "$v" = "$CAP" ] || fail "max_slot_wal_keep_size is '$v', expected $CAP"
pass "max_slot_wal_keep_size lowered to $CAP for the drill"

psql_pod "$leader" "create table if not exists slot_probe(i int)" >/dev/null

echo "cutting $target off from the primary (nftables)"
docker exec "$node" nft add table ip pgha_fence
docker exec "$node" nft "add chain ip pgha_fence isolate { type filter hook forward priority -150 ; policy accept ; }"
docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$leader_ip" ip daddr "$target_ip" counter drop
docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$target_ip" ip daddr "$leader_ip" counter drop
cut_done=yes

echo "generating $ROUNDS WAL segments (about $((ROUNDS * 16)) MB) while $target is cut off"
for i in $(seq 1 "$ROUNDS"); do echo "insert into slot_probe values ($i); select pg_switch_wal();"; done \
  | timeout 180 kubectl -n "$NS" exec -i "$leader" -c postgres -- \
      psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 >/dev/null \
  || fail "the primary could not keep writing while the replica was cut off"
pass "the primary kept accepting writes while $target was cut off"

before_row=$(slot_row)
echo "slot before checkpoint: $before_row"
echo "pg_wal size before checkpoint: $(wal_mb) MB"
case "$before_row" in
  unreserved*|lost*) pass "the slot is past the cap (${before_row%%|*})" ;;
  *) fail "the slot is still within the cap: $before_row" ;;
esac

checkpoint
checkpoint
gone=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  c=$(psql_pod "$leader" "select count(*) from pg_ls_waldir() where name = '$need_file'")
  if [ "$c" = 0 ]; then gone=yes; break; fi
  sleep 2
done
echo "slot after checkpoints: $(slot_row || true)"
[ -n "$gone" ] || fail "the WAL file $need_file the lagging replica needs is still on the primary; retention is not capped"
pass "the WAL file $need_file needed by $target was removed from the primary (retention is capped)"
echo "pg_wal size after checkpoints: $(wal_mb) MB (informational: Postgres keeps recycled segments, so this is not the retention measure)"

echo "removing the cut"
docker exec "$node" nft delete table ip pgha_fence
cut_done=""
recovered=""
st=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  st=$(cluster_json | jq -r --arg m "$target" '.[] | select(.Member == $m) | .State')
  if [ "$st" = streaming ]; then recovered=yes; break; fi
  sleep 4
done
if [ -n "$recovered" ]; then
  echo "OBSERVATION: $target caught up and is streaming again on its own"
else
  echo "OBSERVATION: $target did NOT recover on its own (state: ${st:-unknown}); the WAL it needs was removed and there is no WAL archive yet"
  echo "running: patronictl reinit pg-ha $target (manual step, recorded as a finding)"
  ctl reinit pg-ha "$target" --force >/dev/null
fi
wait_healthy 400 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table
pass "cluster is back to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"

ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG" --force >/dev/null
cap_changed=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  v=$(psql_pod "$leader" "show max_slot_wal_keep_size" || true)
  [ "$v" = "$ORIG" ] && break
  sleep 2
done
[ "$v" = "$ORIG" ] || fail "max_slot_wal_keep_size was not restored (it is '$v')"
pass "max_slot_wal_keep_size restored to $ORIG"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/rep-slot-disk.sh
grep -c "need_file" tests/rep-slot-disk.sh
shellcheck tests/rep-slot-disk.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"
The grep should now print a number above 0, followed by shellcheck OK.

2. Run the drill

3. Check that nothing is left behind

You should see max_slot_wal_keep_size: 1GB and three 0 counts.

hmsmiraz@HMSMIRAZ:~/postgres-ha-lab$ tee tests/rep-slot-disk.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF' #!/usr/bin/env bash # REP-3 drill: a replica that stops consuming WAL must not be able to make the primary keep WAL forever. # max_slot_wal_keep_size is lowered to 128MB for the drill (via Patroni) and restored af

PASTED
The drill failed again at the same place, and I now think I misdiagnosed the first failure, so here is what the evidence supports.

What the output shows
The cap works on the slot. The slot went from reserved to unreserved (safe_wal_size is -251658240, meaning 240 MB over the cap), and writes continued.
The checkpoint is where it breaks, both times. The failing command is the checkpoint, and nothing was printed with it, no ERROR, no message. A client-side timeout produces exactly that: timeout exits silently with code 124 after 180 s. So I think the checkpoint hung, but I have no timestamps, so I haven’t proven it.
I blamed the wrong thing before. The first failure was a 30 s limit, and I guessed disk speed. A 180 s limit failing the same way makes slowness unlikely.
My hypothesis (not confirmed)
To invalidate a slot, a checkpoint has to stop the walsender that holds it. The walsender for pg-ha-2 is stuck talking to a peer behind a silent packet drop, so it may not release the slot. Earlier evidence fits this: the first run’s cleanup removed the cut, and the WAL was then removed and the slot came back as new. The checkpoint could only complete once the connection was gone.

If that’s right, it’s a real finding and not a test bug. During a silent partition of a lagging replica, a checkpoint can block, and WAL then can’t be removed until the walsender dies. The pg_wal growth we saw (449 MB to 513 MB) would be the cost. The cap would still be correct, but it wouldn’t bound the disk while that checkpoint is stuck. I need a measurement before saying so.

1. Check the state first (read-only)
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml show-config | grep max_slot_wal_keep_size
for n in kind-worker kind-worker2 kind-worker3; do echo "$n: $(docker exec $n nft list tables | grep -c pgha_fence)"; done
You should see the cap at 1GB and three 0 counts. If pg-ha-2 shows running with a stuck lag instead of streaming, it needs patronictl reinit again for the same reason as before, which is the WAL it needs being gone:

kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml reinit pg-ha pg-ha-2 --force
Wait until it shows streaming with lag 0 before the next step.

2. Keep the failed run as evidence
mv docs/evidence/REP-3-drill.txt docs/evidence/REP-3-drill-run2-FAILED.txt
3. Observe the checkpoint with timing
This repeats the drill’s setup and starts the checkpoint in the background. Every 10 s for up to 150 s it prints whether the checkpoint finished, the slot, what the checkpointer and walsenders are waiting on, and the pg_wal size. It then removes the cut and waits for the checkpoint to finish. The cut and the cap are always restored:

bash -s <<'EOF'
set -u
NS=pg-ha
ctl() { timeout 60 kubectl -n $NS exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml "$@"; }
q() { timeout 30 kubectl -n $NS exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -c "$2"; }
leader=$(ctl list -f json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role=="Leader") | .Member')
target=$(ctl list -f json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role=="Replica") | .Member' | head -1)
slot=${target//-/_}
node=$(kubectl -n $NS get pod $leader -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
lip=$(kubectl -n $NS get pod $leader -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
tip=$(kubectl -n $NS get pod $target -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
cutdone=""; capdone=""
cleanup() {
  [ -n "$cutdone" ] && docker exec $node nft delete table ip pgha_fence 2>/dev/null && cutdone=""
  [ -n "$capdone" ] && ctl edit-config -p max_slot_wal_keep_size=1GB --force >/dev/null 2>&1 && capdone=""
}
trap cleanup EXIT
echo "leader $leader on $node, drill replica $target (slot $slot)"
echo "wal_sender_timeout = $(q $leader 'show wal_sender_timeout')"
ctl edit-config -p max_slot_wal_keep_size=128MB --force >/dev/null; capdone=yes
sleep 10
echo "cap now: $(q $leader 'show max_slot_wal_keep_size')"
q $leader "create table if not exists slot_probe(i int)" >/dev/null
docker exec $node nft add table ip pgha_fence
docker exec $node nft "add chain ip pgha_fence isolate { type filter hook forward priority -150 ; policy accept ; }"
docker exec $node nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr $lip ip daddr $tip counter drop
docker exec $node nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr $tip ip daddr $lip counter drop
cutdone=yes
echo "cut in place at $(date +%T); generating WAL"
for i in $(seq 1 24); do echo "insert into slot_probe values ($i); select pg_switch_wal();"; done \
  | timeout 180 kubectl -n $NS exec -i $leader -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq >/dev/null
echo "slot: $(q $leader "select wal_status||'|active='||active from pg_replication_slots where slot_name='$slot'")"

echo "checkpoint started at $(date +%T)"

  sleep 10
  echo "== t+$((i*10))s checkpoint: $( [ -f /tmp/ckpt.done ] && cat /tmp/ckpt.done || echo running )"
  echo "   slot: $(q $leader "select wal_status||'|active='||active from pg_replication_slots where slot_name='$slot'")"
  echo "   waits: $(q $leader "select backend_type||'='||coalesce(wait_event_type,'-')||'/'||coalesce(wait_event,'-') from pg_stat_activity where backend_type in ('checkpointer','walsender') order by 1" | tr '\n' ' ')"
  echo "   pg_wal: $(timeout 20 kubectl -n $NS exec $leader -c postgres -- sh -c 'du -sm /pgdata/data/pg_wal | cut -f1') MB"
  [ -f /tmp/ckpt.done ] && break
done
echo "--- removing the cut at $(date +%T) ---"
cleanup
for i in $(seq 1 12); do [ -f /tmp/ckpt.done ] && break; sleep 5; done
echo "checkpoint result: $( [ -f /tmp/ckpt.done ] && cat /tmp/ckpt.done || echo 'STILL RUNNING after the cut was removed')"
echo "checkpoint output: $(cat /tmp/ckpt.out 2>/dev/null)"
echo "slot: $(q $leader "select coalesce(wal_status,'-')||'|active='||active from pg_replication_slots where slot_name='$slot'")"
EOF
It runs for about 3 to 5 minutes. Don’t press anything while it runs.

2. Run the observation
It sets up the same drill, starts the checkpoint in the background, and prints a status line every 10 s with the slot state, what the checkpointer and walsenders are waiting on, and the pg_wal size. It then removes the cut and waits for the checkpoint to finish. The cut and the cap are restored by the script itself, even if you interrupt it:

bash -s <<'EOF'
set -u
NS=pg-ha
ctl() { timeout 60 kubectl -n $NS exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml "$@"; }
q() { timeout 30 kubectl -n $NS exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -c "$2"; }
leader=$(ctl list -f json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role=="Leader") | .Member')
target=$(ctl list -f json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role=="Replica") | .Member' | head -1)
slot=${target//-/_}
node=$(kubectl -n $NS get pod $leader -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
lip=$(kubectl -n $NS get pod $leader -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
tip=$(kubectl -n $NS get pod $target -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
cutdone=""; capdone=""
cleanup() {
  [ -n "$cutdone" ] && docker exec $node nft delete table ip pgha_fence 2>/dev/null && cutdone=""
  [ -n "$capdone" ] && ctl edit-config -p max_slot_wal_keep_size=1GB --force >/dev/null 2>&1 && capdone=""
}
trap cleanup EXIT
echo "leader $leader on $node, drill replica $target (slot $slot)"
echo "wal_sender_timeout = $(q $leader 'show wal_sender_timeout')"
ctl edit-config -p max_slot_wal_keep_size=128MB --force >/dev/null; capdone=yes
sleep 10
echo "cap now: $(q $leader 'show max_slot_wal_keep_size')"
q $leader "create table if not exists slot_probe(i int)" >/dev/null
docker exec $node nft add table ip pgha_fence
docker exec $node nft "add chain ip pgha_fence isolate { type filter hook forward priority -150 ; policy accept ; }"
docker exec $node nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr $lip ip daddr $tip counter drop
docker exec $node nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr $tip ip daddr $lip counter drop
cutdone=yes
echo "cut in place at $(date +%T); generating WAL"
for i in $(seq 1 24); do echo "insert into slot_probe values ($i); select pg_switch_wal();"; done \
  | timeout 180 kubectl -n $NS exec -i $leader -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq >/dev/null
echo "slot: $(q $leader "select wal_status||'|active='||active from pg_replication_slots where slot_name='$slot'")"
rm -f /tmp/ckpt.done /tmp/ckpt.out
( s=$SECONDS; timeout 200 kubectl -n $NS exec $leader -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atq -c checkpoint </dev/null >/tmp/ckpt.out 2>&1; echo "rc=$? after $((SECONDS-s))s" > /tmp/ckpt.done ) &
echo "checkpoint started at $(date +%T)"
for i in $(seq 1 15); do
  sleep 10
  echo "== t+$((i*10))s checkpoint: $( [ -f /tmp/ckpt.done ] && cat /tmp/ckpt.done || echo running )"
  echo "   slot: $(q $leader "select wal_status||'|active='||active from pg_replication_slots where slot_name='$slot'")"
  echo "   waits: $(q $leader "select backend_type||'='||coalesce(wait_event_type,'-')||'/'||coalesce(wait_event,'-') from pg_stat_activity where backend_type in ('checkpointer','walsender') order by 1" | tr '\n' ' ')"
  echo "   pg_wal: $(timeout 20 kubectl -n $NS exec $leader -c postgres -- sh -c 'du -sm /pgdata/data/pg_wal | cut -f1') MB"
  [ -f /tmp/ckpt.done ] && break
done
echo "--- removing the cut at $(date +%T) ---"
cleanup
for i in $(seq 1 12); do [ -f /tmp/ckpt.done ] && break; sleep 5; done
echo "checkpoint result: $( [ -f /tmp/ckpt.done ] && cat /tmp/ckpt.done || echo 'STILL RUNNING after the cut was removed')"
echo "checkpoint output: $(cat /tmp/ckpt.out 2>/dev/null)"
echo "slot: $(q $leader "select coalesce(wal_status,'-')||'|active='||active from pg_replication_slots where slot_name='$slot'")"
EOF

PASTED
The observation confirms the first half of my hypothesis, and it also leaves things unexplained that I should not guess at.

What it shows
The checkpoint blocks behind the lagging walsender. The checkpointer sat on IPC/ReplicationSlotDrop for the whole 150 s. That’s the checkpointer waiting for the walsender holding pg_ha_2 to let go of its slot. The walsender sat on Client/ClientWrite, which means it was blocked writing to the cut-off replica. This ties up with run 1: WAL was only removed after the test’s cleanup removed the cut.
wal_sender_timeout did not rescue it. The setting is 1min, yet nothing happened in 169 s. I don’t know why, and I won’t guess.
Removing the cut didn’t free it either. The checkpoint was still running about 30 s after the network came back, until my client gave up (rc=124, after 200s). The cluster table now shows pg-ha-2 as running with a frozen receive LSN and 384 MB of lag. The slot says active=true, so something still holds it.
pg_wal stayed flat at 545 MB. That’s not growth under load, because the test wasn’t writing during the hang, so I have no growth figure yet.
My concern, not yet measured: Postgres removes old WAL at checkpoints, and a stuck checkpoint prevents that. If the primary keeps taking writes while a replica’s connection is dead, pg_wal may grow without limit. This is the danger the cap is supposed to prevent, so I need to measure it.

What I don’t know right now: my client was killed at 200 s, but the server-side checkpoint may still be stuck on the leader. The first step checks that.

1. Read-only state check
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atc "select backend_type||'='||coalesce(wait_event_type,'-')||'/'||coalesce(wait_event,'-') from pg_stat_activity where backend_type in ('checkpointer','walsender') order by 1"
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atc "select slot_name, active, active_pid, wal_status from pg_replication_slots"
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atc "select pid, application_name, state, sent_lsn, replay_lsn, backend_start from pg_stat_replication"
kubectl -n pg-ha logs pg-ha-2 -c postgres --since=10m | grep -vE '^\s|Traceback|^psycopg2|^connection to server' | tail -12
kubectl -n pg-ha logs pg-ha-0 -c postgres --since=10m | grep -iE "walsender|terminat|checkpoint|timeout" | tail -10
2. Release the stuck walsender (a manual operator action)
Only do this if step 1 still shows ReplicationSlotDrop or ClientWrite, with a non-empty active_pid for pg_ha_2:

pid=$(kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atc "select active_pid from pg_replication_slots where slot_name = 'pg_ha_2'")
echo "stuck walsender pid: $pid"
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atc "select pg_terminate_backend($pid)"
sleep 15
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atc "select backend_type||'='||coalesce(wait_event_type,'-')||'/'||coalesce(wait_event,'-') from pg_stat_activity where backend_type in ('checkpointer','walsender') order by 1"
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list
If pg-ha-2 is streaming afterwards, no reinit is needed. If it stays running with a frozen lag, its WAL is gone and it needs the usual:

kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml reinit pg-ha pg-ha-2 --force
Wait until it shows streaming with lag 0 again.

# REP-3: observed behaviour of a lagging replica behind a silent network cut (2026-11-20)

Setup: max_slot_wal_keep_size lowered to 128MB, replica pg-ha-2 cut off with an nftables drop,
24 WAL segments (about 384 MB) generated, then CHECKPOINT started on the primary.

Observed:
- The slot moved from reserved to unreserved (the cap applies to the slot state).
- The primary kept accepting writes while the replica was cut off.
- The checkpointer waited on IPC/ReplicationSlotDrop for at least 150 s; the walsender of the cut-off
  replica waited on Client/ClientWrite. wal_sender_timeout was 1min and did not end the wait.
- pg_wal stayed at 545 MB during the wait (no writes were generated during that window).
- After the cut was removed, the checkpoint was still running about 30 s later (client timeout at
  200 s), and the replica stayed in state "running" with a frozen receive LSN and 384 MB of lag.
- In an earlier run the WAL needed by the replica was removed only after the cut was removed.

Not yet known: why wal_sender_timeout did not fire; whether pg_wal grows without limit under write
load while the checkpoint is blocked; whether TCP settings (tcp_user_timeout, keepalives) end the wait.
EOF
What happens next
I’m not committing anything yet. The drill gets a new pass condition only once we know the real behaviour. The next experiment, which I’m only proposing and haven’t tested, repeats the drill with tcp_user_timeout set on the primary. That’s a real Postgres setting that closes a connection whose sent data goes unacknowledged. I’ll also write to the primary during the hang and measure pg_wal growth, which is the number that matters.

PASTED
The server log shows I misread the last observation, so here is the corrected picture before the next step.

What actually happened
The checkpoint did complete. The primary’s log says checkpoint complete ... total=210.919 s, finishing at 14:22:08 UTC (20:22 local, the time the test prints). That’s about 43 s after the cut was removed and 12 s after my client gave up at 200 s. So “stuck forever” was wrong, and so was my “removing the cut didn’t free it”. It took 211 s, and my client stopped waiting too early.
That explains all three failed runs. My limits were 30 s, then 180 s, then 200 s, and all of them are shorter than 211 s. The test was at fault each time, not the cluster. My earlier guess that the first failure was disk speed was wrong too.
One thing is still unexplained. 48 ms after the checkpoint began, the primary logged terminating process 4695 to release replication slot "pg_ha_2". That walsender stayed alive for about 211 s anyway, and wal_sender_timeout (1 min) didn’t end it. Waiting on a blocked write to a dead connection is my guess, but I haven’t tested it.
Nothing is stuck now. The checkpointer is idle, no walsender holds pg_ha_2, and active_pid is empty. That’s why my pg_terminate_backend command failed on an empty value. It ran nothing on the server, so it was harmless.
pg-ha-2 still needs reinit. Its log says segment ...3B was removed. The checkpoint log says 0 removed, 25 recycled, and recycled files are renamed for reuse, so they still use disk. The proof that the cap works is that the file the replica needs is gone.
The number I still lack is how much pg_wal grows while a checkpoint is waiting on a dead replica and the primary takes writes. The new test measures it.

1. Heal pg-ha-2
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml reinit pg-ha pg-ha-2 --force
for i in $(seq 1 20); do
  kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list | grep pg-ha-2
  sleep 6
done
Wait until it shows streaming with lag 0.

2. Save a corrected note
This replaces the version from my last message:

tee docs/evidence/REP-3-checkpoint-notes.md > /dev/null <<'EOF'
# REP-3: lagging replica behind a silent network cut (observed 2026-11-20)

Setup: max_slot_wal_keep_size lowered to 128MB, replica pg-ha-2 cut off with an nftables drop,
24 WAL segments (about 384 MB) generated, then CHECKPOINT on the primary.

Observed (server log is UTC; the test prints local time, UTC+6):
- The slot moved from reserved to unreserved; the primary kept accepting writes.
- The checkpointer waited on IPC/ReplicationSlotDrop; the walsender of the cut-off replica waited
  on Client/ClientWrite. 48 ms after the checkpoint started the primary logged "terminating process
  ... to release replication slot", but that walsender stayed alive.
- The checkpoint completed after 210.9 s, about 43 s after the cut was removed:
  "0 WAL file(s) added, 0 removed, 25 recycled".
- wal_sender_timeout was 1min and did not end the wait. Not yet explained.
- The replica could not catch up (its WAL segment was removed) and needed
  `patronictl reinit` (manual step) in both runs. A WAL archive (Phase 3) is the real fix.

Test errors on my side, not cluster faults: the first three attempts used client timeouts of
30 s, 180 s and 200 s, all shorter than the 211 s the checkpoint needed.

Not yet measured: pg_wal growth under writes while the checkpoint waits; see REP-3-drill.txt.
EOF
3. Replace the drill test
The checkpoint now runs in the background with a 600 s limit, and the cut stays in place while the primary keeps writing for 60 s. The test records pg_wal growth, then heals the network and measures how long the checkpoint takes to finish:

tee tests/rep-slot-disk.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# REP-3 drill: a replica that stops consuming WAL must not make the primary keep WAL forever.
# max_slot_wal_keep_size is lowered to 128MB (via Patroni) and restored afterwards.
# The replica is cut off silently (nftables drop). Earlier runs showed the primary's checkpoint
# waits on that replica's walsender (ReplicationSlotDrop) and completes after about 211 s.
# This test measures that wait and the pg_wal growth during it, and checks that the WAL file the
# replica needs is gone from the primary afterwards.
set -Eeuo pipefail
trap 'echo "FAIL: command failed at line $LINENO: $BASH_COMMAND"' ERR
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

CAP=128MB
ORIG=1GB
ROUNDS=24
BLOCK_S=60
HEAL_WAIT_S=240
node=""; cut_done=""; cap_changed=""; ckpt_pid=""
ckpt_dir=$(mktemp -d)

ctl() { timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml "$@"; }
psql_pod() { timeout 30 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c "$2"; }
wal_mb() { timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader" -c postgres -- sh -c 'du -sm /pgdata/data/pg_wal | cut -f1'; }
slot_row() { psql_pod "$leader" "select coalesce(wal_status,'-')||'|'||coalesce(invalidation_reason,'-')||'|active='||active||'|safe_wal_size='||coalesce(safe_wal_size::text,'-') from pg_replication_slots where slot_name = '$slot'"; }

cleanup() {
  if [ -n "$ckpt_pid" ]; then kill "$ckpt_pid" 2>/dev/null || true; fi
  if [ -n "$cut_done" ]; then docker exec "$node" nft delete table ip pgha_fence 2>/dev/null || true; fi
  if [ -n "$cap_changed" ]; then
    echo "cleanup: restoring max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG"
    ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG" --force >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  fi
  rm -rf "$ckpt_dir"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"
leader=$(current_leader)
target=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Replica") | .Member' | head -1)
[ -n "$target" ] || fail "no async replica to use for the drill"
slot=${target//-/_}
node=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$leader" -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
leader_ip=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$leader" -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
target_ip=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$target" -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
need_lsn=$(psql_pod "$target" "select pg_last_wal_replay_lsn()")
need_file=$(psql_pod "$leader" "select pg_walfile_name('$need_lsn')")
echo "leader: $leader on $node ($leader_ip); drill replica: $target ($target_ip), slot $slot"
echo "replica replay position: $need_lsn, WAL file it needs: $need_file"
echo "slot before: $(slot_row)"
echo "pg_wal size before: $(wal_mb) MB"

ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$CAP" --force >/dev/null
cap_changed=yes
v=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  v=$(psql_pod "$leader" "show max_slot_wal_keep_size" || true)
  [ "$v" = "$CAP" ] && break
  sleep 2
done
[ "$v" = "$CAP" ] || fail "max_slot_wal_keep_size is '$v', expected $CAP"
pass "max_slot_wal_keep_size lowered to $CAP for the drill"

psql_pod "$leader" "create table if not exists slot_probe(i int)" >/dev/null

echo "cutting $target off from the primary (nftables) at $(date +%T)"
docker exec "$node" nft add table ip pgha_fence
docker exec "$node" nft "add chain ip pgha_fence isolate { type filter hook forward priority -150 ; policy accept ; }"
docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$leader_ip" ip daddr "$target_ip" counter drop
docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$target_ip" ip daddr "$leader_ip" counter drop
cut_done=yes

echo "generating $ROUNDS WAL segments (about $((ROUNDS * 16)) MB) while $target is cut off"
for i in $(seq 1 "$ROUNDS"); do echo "insert into slot_probe values ($i); select pg_switch_wal();"; done \
  | timeout 180 kubectl -n "$NS" exec -i "$leader" -c postgres -- \
      psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 >/dev/null \
  || fail "the primary could not keep writing while the replica was cut off"
pass "the primary kept accepting writes while $target was cut off"

before_row=$(slot_row)
echo "slot after the writes: $before_row"
case "$before_row" in
  unreserved*|lost*) pass "the slot is past the cap (${before_row%%|*})" ;;
  *) fail "the slot is still within the cap: $before_row" ;;
esac

echo "starting CHECKPOINT in the background (client limit 600 s) at $(date +%T)"
(
  s=$SECONDS; rc=0
  timeout 600 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader" -c postgres -- \
    psql -U postgres -Atq -c checkpoint </dev/null >"$ckpt_dir/out" 2>&1 || rc=$?
  echo "rc=$rc after $((SECONDS - s))s" > "$ckpt_dir/done"
) &
ckpt_pid=$!
sleep 5
blocked=$(psql_pod "$leader" "select count(*) from pg_stat_activity where backend_type = 'checkpointer' and wait_event = 'ReplicationSlotDrop'")
echo "checkpointer waiting on ReplicationSlotDrop: $blocked (1 = yes)"
size_start=$(wal_mb)
echo "pg_wal size when the checkpoint started: ${size_start} MB"

echo "primary keeps writing for ${BLOCK_S} s while the checkpoint waits (the cut stays in place)"
t0=$SECONDS; w=0
while [ $((SECONDS - t0)) -lt "$BLOCK_S" ]; do
  w=$((w + 1))
  psql_pod "$leader" "insert into slot_probe values ($((1000 + w))); select pg_switch_wal();" >/dev/null \
    || fail "the primary stopped accepting writes while the checkpoint was waiting"
  sleep 4
done
size_block=$(wal_mb)
if [ -f "$ckpt_dir/done" ]; then echo "NOTE: the checkpoint finished by itself during the cut: $(cat "$ckpt_dir/done")"; fi
pass "the primary kept accepting writes during the wait ($w segments of 16 MB written)"
echo "MEASURED: pg_wal grew from ${size_start} MB to ${size_block} MB in ${BLOCK_S} s while the checkpoint waited"

echo "removing the cut at $(date +%T)"
docker exec "$node" nft delete table ip pgha_fence
cut_done=""
heal_at=$SECONDS
done_ok=""
for _ in $(seq 1 "$HEAL_WAIT_S"); do
  if [ -f "$ckpt_dir/done" ]; then done_ok=yes; break; fi
  sleep 1
done
echo "checkpoint (client view): $(cat "$ckpt_dir/done" 2>/dev/null || echo 'still running')"
[ -n "$done_ok" ] || fail "the checkpoint did not finish within ${HEAL_WAIT_S} s after the cut was removed"
echo "MEASURED: seconds from removing the cut to checkpoint completion: $((SECONDS - heal_at))"
grep -q 'rc=0' "$ckpt_dir/done" || fail "the checkpoint command failed: $(cat "$ckpt_dir/out")"
wait "$ckpt_pid" 2>/dev/null || true
ckpt_pid=""
pass "the checkpoint completed once the dead connection was gone"

gone=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  c=$(psql_pod "$leader" "select count(*) from pg_ls_waldir() where name = '$need_file'")
  if [ "$c" = 0 ]; then gone=yes; break; fi
  sleep 2
done
echo "slot after the checkpoint: $(slot_row || true)"
[ -n "$gone" ] || fail "the WAL file $need_file the lagging replica needs is still on the primary; retention is not capped"
pass "the WAL file $need_file needed by $target is gone from the primary (retention is capped)"
echo "pg_wal size now: $(wal_mb) MB (informational: Postgres keeps recycled segments)"

recovered=""
st=""
for _ in $(seq 1 15); do
  st=$(cluster_json | jq -r --arg m "$target" '.[] | select(.Member == $m) | .State')
  if [ "$st" = streaming ]; then recovered=yes; break; fi
  sleep 4
done
if [ -n "$recovered" ]; then
  echo "OBSERVATION: $target caught up and is streaming again on its own"
else
  echo "OBSERVATION: $target did NOT recover on its own (state: ${st:-unknown}); the WAL it needs was removed and there is no WAL archive yet"
  echo "running: patronictl reinit pg-ha $target (manual step, recorded as a finding)"
  ctl reinit pg-ha "$target" --force >/dev/null
fi
wait_healthy 400 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table
pass "cluster is back to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"

ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG" --force >/dev/null
cap_changed=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  v=$(psql_pod "$leader" "show max_slot_wal_keep_size" || true)
  [ "$v" = "$ORIG" ] && break
  sleep 2
done
[ "$v" = "$ORIG" ] || fail "max_slot_wal_keep_size was not restored (it is '$v')"
pass "max_slot_wal_keep_size restored to $ORIG"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
shellcheck tests/rep-slot-disk.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"
4. Run it
Run it once step 1 shows pg-ha-2 streaming. It takes about 6 to 9 minutes, and the wait for the checkpoint is the long part. Don’t press anything while it runs. The test removes its nft table and restores the 1 GB setting itself, even if it fails:

bash tests/rep-slot-disk.sh 2>&1 | tee docs/evidence/REP-3-drill.txt
5. Check that nothing is left behind
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml show-config | grep max_slot_wal_keep_size
for n in kind-worker kind-worker2 kind-worker3; do echo "$n: $(docker exec $n nft list tables | grep -c pgha_fence)"; done
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list
You should see 1GB, three 0 counts, and 1 leader with 2 streaming replicas.
