What decides the next step
Passes: the file the replica needs is gone, writes continued, and we get two measurements (the pg_wal growth and the time to finish after healing). Both go into the write-up, and the reinit need goes into known limitations until the archive exists.
The MEASURED: pg_wal grew line shows large growth: that’s the real risk of a silent partition, and I’ll document it with the number and propose a fix (tcp_user_timeout on the primary) as a separate, tested change.
the checkpoint did not finish within 240 s: a different behaviour from what the log shows, and I’ll look at it before changing anything.
A FAIL: command failed at line N message: that names the cause, and I’ll fix the test from it.

The full output of the drill, in particular the two MEASURED: lines, the checkpointer waiting on ReplicationSlotDrop line, and the OBSERVATION line.

# REP-3 drill: a replica that stops consuming WAL must not make the primary keep WAL forever.
# max_slot_wal_keep_size is lowered to 128MB (via Patroni) and restored afterwards.
# The replica is cut off silently (nftables drop). Earlier runs showed the primary's checkpoint
# waits on that replica's walsender (ReplicationSlotDrop) and completes after about 211 s.
# This test measures that wait and the pg_wal growth during it, and checks that the WAL file the
# replica needs is gone from the primary afterwards.

trap 'echo "FAIL: command failed at line $LINENO: $BASH_COMMAND"' ERR
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

CAP=128MB
ORIG=1GB
ROUNDS=24
BLOCK_S=60
HEAL_WAIT_S=240
node=""; cut_done=""; cap_changed=""; ckpt_pid=""
ckpt_dir=$(mktemp -d)

ctl() { timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml "$@"; }

  if [ -n "$cap_changed" ]; then
    echo "cleanup: restoring max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG"
    ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG" --force >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  fi
  rm -rf "$ckpt_dir"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"

echo "leader: $leader on $node ($leader_ip); drill replica: $target ($target_ip), slot $slot"

  [ "$v" = "$CAP" ] && break
  sleep 2
done

pass "max_slot_wal_keep_size lowered to $CAP for the drill"

docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$target_ip" ip daddr "$leader_ip" counter drop

  | timeout 180 kubectl -n "$NS" exec -i "$leader" -c postgres -- \
      psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 >/dev/null \
  || fail "the primary could not keep writing while the replica was cut off"

before_row=$(slot_row)

  unreserved*|lost*) pass "the slot is past the cap (${before_row%%|*})" ;;
  *) fail "the slot is still within the cap: $before_row" ;;
esac

echo "starting CHECKPOINT in the background (client limit 600 s) at $(date +%T)"
(
  s=$SECONDS; rc=0
  timeout 600 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader" -c postgres -- \
    psql -U postgres -Atq -c checkpoint </dev/null >"$ckpt_dir/out" 2>&1 || rc=$?
  echo "rc=$rc after $((SECONDS - s))s" > "$ckpt_dir/done"
) &
ckpt_pid=$!
sleep 5
blocked=$(psql_pod "$leader" "select count(*) from pg_stat_activity where backend_type = 'checkpointer' and wait_event = 'ReplicationSlotDrop'")
echo "checkpointer waiting on ReplicationSlotDrop: $blocked (1 = yes)"

echo "pg_wal size when the checkpoint started: ${size_start} MB"

while [ $((SECONDS - t0)) -lt "$BLOCK_S" ]; do
  w=$((w + 1))
  psql_pod "$leader" "insert into slot_probe values ($((1000 + w))); select pg_switch_wal();" >/dev/null \
    || fail "the primary stopped accepting writes while the checkpoint was waiting"
  sleep 4
done
size_block=$(wal_mb)
if [ -f "$ckpt_dir/done" ]; then echo "NOTE: the checkpoint finished by itself during the cut: $(cat "$ckpt_dir/done")"; fi
pass "the primary kept accepting writes during the wait ($w segments of 16 MB written)"
echo "MEASURED: pg_wal grew from ${size_start} MB to ${size_block} MB in ${BLOCK_S} s while the checkpoint waited"

echo "removing the cut at $(date +%T)"
docker exec "$node" nft delete table ip pgha_fence
cut_done=""
heal_at=$SECONDS
done_ok=""
for _ in $(seq 1 "$HEAL_WAIT_S"); do
  if [ -f "$ckpt_dir/done" ]; then done_ok=yes; break; fi
  sleep 1
done
echo "checkpoint (client view): $(cat "$ckpt_dir/done" 2>/dev/null || echo 'still running')"
[ -n "$done_ok" ] || fail "the checkpoint did not finish within ${HEAL_WAIT_S} s after the cut was removed"
echo "MEASURED: seconds from removing the cut to checkpoint completion: $((SECONDS - heal_at))"
grep -q 'rc=0' "$ckpt_dir/done" || fail "the checkpoint command failed: $(cat "$ckpt_dir/out")"
wait "$ckpt_pid" 2>/dev/null || true
ckpt_pid=""
pass "the checkpoint completed once the dead connection was gone"

gone=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  c=$(psql_pod "$leader" "select count(*) from pg_ls_waldir() where name = '$need_file'")
  if [ "$c" = 0 ]; then gone=yes; break; fi
  sleep 2
done
echo "slot after the checkpoint: $(slot_row || true)"
[ -n "$gone" ] || fail "the WAL file $need_file the lagging replica needs is still on the primary; retention is not capped"
pass "the WAL file $need_file needed by $target is gone from the primary (retention is capped)"
echo "pg_wal size now: $(wal_mb) MB (informational: Postgres keeps recycled segments)"

recovered=""
st=""
for _ in $(seq 1 15); do
  st=$(cluster_json | jq -r --arg m "$target" '.[] | select(.Member == $m) | .State')
  if [ "$st" = streaming ]; then recovered=yes; break; fi
  sleep 4
done
if [ -n "$recovered" ]; then
  echo "OBSERVATION: $target caught up and is streaming again on its own"
else
  echo "OBSERVATION: $target did NOT recover on its own (state: ${st:-unknown}); the WAL it needs was removed and there is no WAL archive yet"
  echo "running: patronictl reinit pg-ha $target (manual step, recorded as a finding)"
  ctl reinit pg-ha "$target" --force >/dev/null
fi
wait_healthy 400 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table
pass "cluster is back to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"

ctl edit-config -p "max_slot_wal_keep_size=$ORIG" --force >/dev/null
cap_changed=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  v=$(psql_pod "$leader" "show max_slot_wal_keep_size" || true)
  [ "$v" = "$ORIG" ] && break
  sleep 2
done
[ "$v" = "$ORIG" ] || fail "max_slot_wal_keep_size was not restored (it is '$v')"
pass "max_slot_wal_keep_size restored to $ORIG"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
grep -c "BLOCK_S" tests/rep-slot-disk.sh
shellcheck tests/rep-slot-disk.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"
The grep should now print a number above 0, followed by shellcheck OK.

msmiraz@HMSMIRAZ:~/postgres-ha-lab$ tee tests/rep-slot-disk.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF' #!/usr/bin/env bash # REP-3 drill: a replica that stops consuming WAL must not make the primary keep WAL forever. # max_slot_wal_keep_size is lowered to 128MB (via Patroni) and restored afterwards. # The replica i

PASTED
The REP-3 drill passed (ALL CHECKS PASSED), and the two measurements show a limit of the disk protection that has to go into the write-up.

What it proved
The cap works on the slot. The slot went from reserved to unreserved, and after the checkpoint the WAL file the replica needed (...55) was gone from the primary.
Writes never stopped. The primary accepted all 13 segments of writes during the wait.
The checkpoint did block behind the dead replica. The test saw the checkpointer on ReplicationSlotDrop (1), as the earlier log suggested.
What it measured
pg_wal grew from 545 MB to 753 MB in 60 s, which is 208 MB. The test wrote 13 segments of 16 MB, also 208 MB. So while the checkpoint waits, nothing is removed and the disk grows by exactly the volume written. The 128 MB cap didn’t bound anything in that window. It only takes effect after the checkpoint completes.
Time to finish after the cut was removed: 37 s (43 s in the earlier run). The two runs agree, but I don’t know why it takes about 40 s, and I won’t guess.
wal_sender_timeout (1 min) did not end the wait. The checkpoint had waited about 66 s before I removed the cut.
What that means for the brief. It asks to protect against slots filling the disk. The cap does that for a replica that disconnects normally. For a silent network partition, protection arrives only when the walsender gives up, and the disk can grow until then. I measured 60 s, and I haven’t measured a longer partition, so I can’t say how bad it gets. The honest statement is the one above, plus an alert on pg_wal size in Phase 4.

The replica didn’t recover on its own. It needed patronictl reinit for the third time, which is the same gap a WAL archive closes in Phase 3.

1. Add the measurements to the notes
tee -a docs/evidence/REP-3-checkpoint-notes.md > /dev/null <<'EOF'

## Run 3 (tests/rep-slot-disk.sh, evidence in REP-3-drill.txt)
- Checkpointer confirmed waiting on ReplicationSlotDrop while the replica was cut off.
- pg_wal grew from 545 MB to 753 MB in 60 s while the primary wrote 13 segments of 16 MB (208 MB):
  growth equals the volume written. The 128MB cap did not bound pg_wal during the wait.
- The checkpoint completed 37 s after the cut was removed (43 s in the earlier run). Cause of the
  ~40 s not established.
- The WAL file the replica needed (000000070000000000000055) was gone afterwards; the replica
  could not recover and needed `patronictl reinit` (third occurrence).
- Not tested: longer partitions; tcp_user_timeout or keepalive settings on the primary as a fix.
EOF
2. Start docs/known-limitations.md
It lists only what we observed or decided so far, and we’ll extend it as we go:

tee docs/known-limitations.md > /dev/null <<'EOF'
# Known limitations

Each item says how it was found. Items marked "not tested" are open questions, not claims.

## Backup and recovery
- No WAL archive or base backups exist yet (Phase 3). Until then a replica that falls behind past
  `max_slot_wal_keep_size` cannot catch up and needs `patronictl reinit` (manual). Observed 3 times
  (REP-3 drill, runs 1 to 3).

## Replication and disk
- During a silent network partition of a lagging replica, the primary's checkpoint waits on that
  replica's walsender (ReplicationSlotDrop) and `pg_wal` grows by the volume written, because no WAL
  is removed until the checkpoint completes. Measured: 545 MB to 753 MB in 60 s with 208 MB written.
  `wal_sender_timeout` (1 min) did not end the wait. The cap bounds retention only after the
  checkpoint completes. Longer partitions were not tested. Mitigations not tested yet:
  `tcp_user_timeout` on the primary; an alert on `pg_wal` size (Phase 4); PVC sizing.

## Failover and fencing
- Fencing is software only (leader lock plus self-demotion). kind has no hardware watchdog, so a
  hung Patroni process on a partitioned node is not covered (ADR-0003).
- The split-brain test samples the three pods about 0.3 s apart. It shows no sustained overlap of
  two writable primaries; it is not a proof for single instants. The guarantee rests on the lock design.
- A crashed primary pod can restart in place before the lock expires, so no promotion happens
  (FO-1, RTO 6.7 s). A promotion is forced only by node loss (FO-2) or storage loss (FO-3).
- FO-2, first run: all 44 acknowledged writes were reported missing. The rerun passed with the same
  data and a more careful read. The first run's probe log was not saved, so the cause is unproven
  (my best guess is a read error in the test helper). Kept as FO-2-run1-FAILED.txt.

## Configuration
- The `pg_hba` rules exist twice: in the local Patroni file (new clusters) and in the DCS config
  (set with `patronictl edit-config`). They match today; which one is effective was not tested.

## Environment
- All "nodes" run on one laptop (kind), so failures are simulated, not independent. Zones are labels.
- Node loss is `docker kill` of a kind node; network partitions are nftables drops on the node.
EOF

hmsmiraz@HMSMIRAZ:~/postgres-ha-lab$ tee -a docs/evidence/REP-3-checkpoint-notes.md > /dev/null <<'EOF' ## Run 3 (tests/rep-slot-disk.sh, evidence in REP-3-drill.txt) - Checkpointer confirmed waiting on ReplicationSlotDrop while the replica was cut off. - pg_wal grew from 545 MB to 753 MB in 60

PASTED
The commit is pushed (aeee276) and gitleaks found no leaks. The REP-3 drill, its notes and docs/known-limitations.md are now in the repo.

Phase 2, step 10: degraded synchronous mode (ADR-0001)
ADR-0001 makes three claims that we haven’t measured yet:

RPO is 0 while a sync standby exists. REP-2 showed the mechanism, but nothing has shown it surviving a failover.
Patroni falls back to asynchronous when no sync standby is reachable, so the primary keeps accepting writes.
In that degraded state RPO is above 0.
What the test does:

A writer inside the primary’s pod inserts one row every 0.5 s with default synchronous commit and no timeout, so a blocked commit shows up as a gap in the acknowledgements.
The test cuts the primary off from both replicas with the same nft method as FO-5. API access stays up, so the lock isn’t lost.
It polls synchronous_standby_names to measure how long commits stay blocked until Patroni falls back to asynchronous. It fails if that doesn’t happen within 300 s.
It checks that writes resume after the fall back and that the replicas stay behind the primary.
It powers off the primary’s node and waits for a promotion.
It compares every acknowledged row with what exists on the new primary.
What I’m asserting, and what I’m only recording:

Asserted: no row acknowledged before the cut is lost (the RPO 0 claim), and synchronous mode comes back on its own once replicas return.
Recorded as a finding: how many rows acknowledged after the fall back are lost. I expect some, but that’s a prediction, so the test prints the number and doesn’t require it.
A risk, unmeasured. Earlier, a walsender behind a silent drop stayed alive for 3 minutes or more despite wal_sender_timeout = 1min. Patroni may see the replicas as still streaming for that long, and then commits would stay blocked. That would be a real finding about silent partitions, and the test would fail with that message and be kept as evidence.

1. Write the test
tee tests/rep-degraded-sync.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ADR-0001, degraded case: no synchronous standby is reachable.
# Measures (1) how long commits block, (2) whether Patroni falls back to asynchronous so writes resume,
# (3) which acknowledged writes are lost if the primary is then lost, (4) that sync mode comes back.
set -Eeuo pipefail
trap 'echo "FAIL: command failed at line $LINENO: $BASH_COMMAND"' ERR
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

MAX_DEGRADE_S=300
node=""; cut_done=""; writer_pid=""
peers=(); peer_pods=()
WDIR=$(mktemp -d)

psql_pod() { timeout 30 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c "$2"; }
acks() { grep -c '^ack' "$WDIR/writer.log" 2>/dev/null || true; }

cut() {
  local ip
  docker exec "$node" nft add table ip pgha_fence || return 1
  docker exec "$node" nft "add chain ip pgha_fence isolate { type filter hook forward priority -150 ; policy accept ; }" || return 1
  for ip in "${peers[@]}"; do
    docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$leader_ip" ip daddr "$ip" counter drop || return 1
    docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$ip" ip daddr "$leader_ip" counter drop || return 1
  done
  cut_done=yes
}

cleanup() {
  if [ -n "$writer_pid" ]; then kill "$writer_pid" 2>/dev/null || true; fi
  if [ -n "$node" ]; then
    if [ "$(docker inspect -f '{{.State.Running}}' "$node" 2>/dev/null || echo false)" != "true" ]; then
      echo "cleanup: starting $node again"
      docker start "$node" >/dev/null || true
    elif [ -n "$cut_done" ]; then
      echo "cleanup: removing the replication cut"
      docker exec "$node" nft delete table ip pgha_fence 2>/dev/null || true
    fi
  fi
  rm -rf "$WDIR"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"
leader=$(current_leader)
node=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$leader" -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
leader_ip=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$leader" -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
tl_before=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')
pods_json=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pods -l application=pg-ha,cluster-name=pg-ha -o json)
mapfile -t peers < <(jq -r --arg o "$leader" '.items[] | select(.metadata.name != $o) | .status.podIP' <<<"$pods_json")
mapfile -t peer_pods < <(jq -r --arg o "$leader" '.items[] | select(.metadata.name != $o) | .metadata.name' <<<"$pods_json")
sync_before=$(psql_pod "$leader" "show synchronous_standby_names")
echo "leader: $leader on $node; replicas: ${peer_pods[*]}; synchronous_standby_names before: $sync_before"
[ -n "$sync_before" ] || fail "synchronous mode is not active before the test"

psql_pod "$leader" "drop table if exists degraded_probe" >/dev/null 2>&1
psql_pod "$leader" "create table degraded_probe(n int primary key)" >/dev/null

# The writer runs inside the primary's pod: synchronous_commit on (default), no statement timeout.
# A blocked commit therefore shows up as a gap between two "ack" lines.
# shellcheck disable=SC2016
timeout 1500 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader" -c postgres -- bash -c '
  i=0
  while true; do
    i=$((i + 1))
    if psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c "insert into degraded_probe(n) values ($i)" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
      echo "ack $i $(date +%s%3N)"
    else
      echo "err $i $(date +%s%3N)"
    fi
    sleep 0.5
  done' >"$WDIR/writer.log" 2>/dev/null &
writer_pid=$!
sleep 6
[ "$(acks)" -ge 5 ] || fail "the writer is not acknowledging writes before the cut ($(acks))"
pass "writer acknowledging writes before the cut ($(acks) acks)"

cut || fail "the nft rules could not be installed, so there is no cut to test"
cut_ms=$(now_ms)
echo "cut: $leader is cut off from ${peer_pods[*]} (API access stays up) at $(date +%T)"

degraded_ms=""
t0=$SECONDS
while [ $((SECONDS - t0)) -lt "$MAX_DEGRADE_S" ]; do
  sleep 5
  names=""; ok=""
  if names=$(psql_pod "$leader" "show synchronous_standby_names" 2>/dev/null); then ok=yes; fi
  echo "t+$((SECONDS - t0))s synchronous_standby_names='${names}' acks=$(acks)"
  if [ -n "$ok" ] && [ -z "$names" ]; then degraded_ms=$(now_ms); break; fi
done
[ -n "$degraded_ms" ] || fail "Patroni did not fall back to asynchronous within ${MAX_DEGRADE_S} s; commits stay blocked while no sync standby is reachable (the ADR-0001 assumption does not hold)"
blocked_s=$(((degraded_ms - cut_ms) / 1000))
echo "MEASURED: Patroni switched to asynchronous (empty synchronous_standby_names) ${blocked_s} s after the cut"
pass "Patroni fell back to asynchronous replication on its own"

a0=$(acks)
sleep 15
a1=$(acks)
echo "acknowledged writes in the 15 s after the switch: $((a1 - a0))"
[ $((a1 - a0)) -ge 5 ] || fail "writes did not resume after the switch to asynchronous"
pass "writes resumed after the fall back (the primary stays available)"
max_gap=$(awk '$1=="ack" { if (prev) { g=$3-prev; if (g>m) m=g } prev=$3 } END { print m+0 }' "$WDIR/writer.log")
echo "MEASURED: longest gap between acknowledged writes: ${max_gap} ms"

leader_n=$(psql_pod "$leader" "select count(*) from degraded_probe")
for p in "${peer_pods[@]}"; do
  c=$(psql_pod "$p" "select count(*) from degraded_probe")
  echo "rows on $p: $c, rows on the primary $leader: $leader_n"
  [ "$c" -lt "$leader_n" ] || fail "replica $p has all the rows; the cut did not isolate it"
done
pass "the replicas are behind: writes acknowledged in the degraded state exist only on the primary"

echo "powering off node $node (docker kill) at $(date +%T)"
docker kill "$node" >/dev/null
cut_done=""
sleep 1
kill "$writer_pid" 2>/dev/null || true
writer_pid=""
cp "$WDIR/writer.log" docs/evidence/ADR1-degraded-writer.log

new=""
deadline=$((SECONDS + 180))
while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
  new=$(current_leader 2>/dev/null || true)
  if [ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$leader" ]; then break; fi
  sleep 3
done
echo "leader after: $new"
[ -n "$new" ] && [ "$new" != "$leader" ] || fail "no replica was promoted"
pass "replica $new was promoted"

psql_pod "$new" "select n from degraded_probe" > "$WDIR/present.txt"
[ -s "$WDIR/present.txt" ] || fail "could not read degraded_probe on the new primary"
acked_total=$(awk '$1=="ack"' "$WDIR/writer.log" | wc -l)
awk 'NR==FNR { p[$1]=1; next } $1=="ack" && !($2 in p) { print $2, $3 }' "$WDIR/present.txt" "$WDIR/writer.log" > "$WDIR/lost.txt"
lost=$(wc -l < "$WDIR/lost.txt")
lost_before=$(awk -v c="$cut_ms" '$2 <= c' "$WDIR/lost.txt" | wc -l)
echo "acknowledged writes in total: $acked_total; present on the new primary: $(wc -l < "$WDIR/present.txt"); lost: $lost"
[ "$lost_before" = 0 ] || fail "$lost_before write(s) acknowledged BEFORE the cut were lost (the RPO 0 claim is broken)"
pass "no write acknowledged while the synchronous standby was healthy was lost (RPO 0 holds)"
if [ "$lost" -gt 0 ]; then
  window_ms=$(awk 'NR==1 { f=$2 } { l=$2 } END { print l - f }' "$WDIR/lost.txt")
  echo "FINDING (as ADR-0001 predicts): $lost write(s) acknowledged after the fall back to asynchronous were lost; they span ${window_ms} ms of acknowledgements"
else
  echo "NOTE: no acknowledged write was lost in this run"
fi

echo "starting node $node again"
docker start "$node" >/dev/null
wait_healthy 600 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table
new=$(current_leader)
present_n=$(wc -l < "$WDIR/present.txt")
for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
  ok=""
  for _ in $(seq 1 10); do
    c=$(psql_pod "$p" "select count(*) from degraded_probe" || true)
    if [ "$c" = "$present_n" ]; then ok=yes; break; fi
    sleep 3
  done
  echo "rows on $p: ${c:-?} (expected $present_n)"
  [ -n "$ok" ] || fail "$p does not match the new primary after the rejoin"
done
pass "all three nodes agree after the old primary rejoined (the lost rows are gone everywhere)"

restored=""
names=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  names=$(psql_pod "$new" "show synchronous_standby_names" || true)
  if [ -n "$names" ]; then restored=yes; break; fi
  sleep 4
done
[ -n "$restored" ] || fail "synchronous mode did not come back after the replicas returned"
pass "synchronous mode came back on its own (synchronous_standby_names=$names)"

tl_after=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .TL')
[ "$tl_after" -gt "$tl_before" ] || fail "timeline did not advance"
pass "timeline advanced ($tl_before -> $tl_after)"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/rep-degraded-sync.sh
shellcheck tests/rep-degraded-sync.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"

Couldn’t read the page
I couldn’t open the repo, because GitHub blocks automated access, so I can’t compare it with what’s committed. I’m working from the files we created together, and the diagnostics below will check the live cluster instead.

What the output shows
Commits blocked for 61 s after the cut. The longest gap between acknowledged writes was 61,056 ms. That matches wal_sender_timeout = 1min, so I suspect Patroni noticed the dead replicas only when the walsenders timed out.
Patroni then fell back to asynchronous on its own. synchronous_standby_names became empty, and writes resumed (25 acknowledged writes in 15 s). That part of ADR-0001 is measured now.
The replicas fell behind as expected. They had 12 and 13 rows while the primary had 40.
After the node was powered off, nothing was promoted. The test waited 180 s and then failed with no replica was promoted.
My reading of the failure, with confidence levels
Likely, from how Patroni’s synchronous mode is designed (not yet confirmed here): with synchronous_mode: true, Patroni only promotes the node that is the current sync standby. After the fall back there is no sync standby, so there is no node it is allowed to promote. That means refusing to fail over is a data-protection choice, not a malfunction. If so, the 40 acknowledged rows survive on the dead primary’s disk, and no write is lost, but the cluster stays down until that node returns.
My ADR was incomplete. ADR-0001 said that Patroni falls back to asynchronous when every replica is gone, so the primary keeps working. I never looked at what that does to failover. My prediction that “some acknowledged writes will be lost” was probably wrong too, because without a promotion no data is lost.
The leader after: pg-ha-0 line may be misleading. A fresh patronictl call can display a stale leader record as alive, so I won’t trust it. The checks below look at the pods directly.
1. Confirm with read-only checks (don’t rerun the test yet)
kubectl get nodes
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-1 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list
for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do echo "$p: in_recovery=$(kubectl -n pg-ha exec $p -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atc 'select pg_is_in_recovery()') rows=$(kubectl -n pg-ha exec $p -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atc 'select count(*) from degraded_probe')"; done
kubectl -n pg-ha get configmap pg-ha-sync -o jsonpath='{.metadata.annotations}'; echo
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-1 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml show-config
2. Read what the replicas decided at the time
The test killed the node at 14:42 UTC, and the server logs use UTC:

for p in pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do echo "== $p"; kubectl -n pg-ha logs $p -c postgres --since=60m | grep -vE '^\s|Traceback|^psycopg2' | grep -E " 14:4[2-9]" | grep -iE "sync|promot|failover|leader|race|lock|healthy" | head -25; done
The log lines are the evidence. A line that says a candidate isn’t promoted because it is not the sync standby would confirm the reading above. If I read something different, I’ll correct my explanation before changing anything.

3. The permanent fix, after the logs
The cluster config isn’t broken. The test asked for something the design deliberately refuses. The fix has three parts, and I’ll write them only after the logs:

Make the test assert what the design guarantees:
commits block for about the wal_sender_timeout and then fall back to async;
with no sync standby, a lost primary is not replaced, and no acknowledged write is lost;
once the node returns, all acknowledged rows are still there, and sync mode comes back.
Correct ADR-0001 with the measured 61 s, the refusal to fail over while degraded, and the trade-off. The RPO is 0 for acknowledged writes, and the cost is no automatic failover while no sync standby is reachable.
Add this to known-limitations.md.
There’s a design decision for you here. The behaviour above is the safe option, and I’d keep it. The alternatives:

Strict mode (synchronous_mode_strict: true) makes writes block instead of falling back to async. It’s safer for data and worse for availability.
Synchronous mode off gives failover in every case, but an RPO above 0.
Shortening the 61 s stall is a separate tuning (wal_sender_timeout), and I’d only propose it as a tested experiment.

Send me
The output of step 1 and step 2. I’m looking for whether any pod promoted itself, whether the 40 rows survive on pg-ha-0, the sync annotations, and the lines that say why the replicas stayed replicas.

PASTED
The logs show that both replicas refused to take over, but not why. Before I say anything more about the cause, I’ll check Patroni’s documentation on what stops a node from counting as “healthiest” in synchronous mode.


Searched the web
Searched the web
Your cluster has no fault, and nothing was lost. The test expected a promotion that the config deliberately refuses. The documentation matches that behaviour, and your output shows the data survived.

What the evidence shows
No data was lost. After the node returned, all three pods hold the same 42 rows. pg-ha-0 is the writable leader on timeline 8, and the sync key is back to sync_standby: pg-ha-1.
The replicas refused to take over. From about 23 s after the kill (about when the 30 s lock TTL ran out) until the test restarted the node, both logged following a different leader because i am not the healthiest node, every 10 s.
The documentation describes exactly this. When no suitable standby is available and the primary fails in synchronous mode, no standby is promoted, and the old primary is promoted automatically when it comes back. The Patroni docs also say a node that is neither the leader nor the current synchronous standby may not promote itself automatically. That fits what happened here: Patroni had dropped the sync standby when it fell back to async. 
debian
readthedocs
What isn’t proven. The log line doesn’t name the reason. My explanation rests on the documentation plus the matching outcome. It’s not a Patroni log line that says so.

What I got wrong
ADR-0001 was incomplete. It described the fall back to async and never asked what that does to failover.
My prediction that some acknowledged writes would be lost was wrong. Patroni protected them by refusing to promote.
The brief’s requirement is met in the normal case. FO-1 to FO-5 all had a synchronous standby available. This test covers a double failure, and the design chooses safety over availability there.
The permanent fix
It has no config change. The config is correct for the RPO we claim, so the test and the documents are what change:

The test now asserts the real contract: commits stall and then fall back to async; no replica promotes itself without a primary and a sync standby; and every acknowledged row survives once the old primary returns.
ADR-0001 gets an amendment with the measured numbers and the trade-off.
known-limitations.md gets the same facts.
A decision for you. I recommend keeping the current behaviour, because it gives RPO 0 as long as the primary’s storage survives, and the cost is no automatic failover while degraded. The alternatives are synchronous_mode_strict: true (writes block instead of falling back) and synchronous_mode: false (failover always, but RPO above 0). Tell me if you prefer one of them.

2. Replace the test
tee tests/rep-degraded-sync.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ADR-0001, degraded case: no synchronous standby is reachable, then the primary is lost.
# Contract (Patroni synchronous mode, non-strict):
#  1. commits stall until Patroni switches to asynchronous (about wal_sender_timeout), then resume;
#  2. with no sync standby and no primary, no replica promotes itself (data protection);
#  3. when the old primary's node returns, every acknowledged write is still there;
#  4. synchronous mode comes back on its own.
set -Eeuo pipefail
trap 'echo "FAIL: command failed at line $LINENO: $BASH_COMMAND"' ERR
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

MAX_DEGRADE_S=180
HOLD_S=90
node=""; cut_done=""; writer_pid=""
peers=(); peer_pods=()
WDIR=$(mktemp -d)

psql_pod() { timeout 30 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c "$2"; }
acks() { grep -c '^ack' "$WDIR/writer.log" 2>/dev/null || true; }
in_recovery() { timeout 15 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atqc "select pg_is_in_recovery()" 2>/dev/null || true; }
writable_pod() {
  local p
  for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    if [ "$(in_recovery "$p")" = "f" ]; then echo "$p"; return 0; fi
  done
  return 1
}

cut() {
  local ip
  docker exec "$node" nft add table ip pgha_fence || return 1
  docker exec "$node" nft "add chain ip pgha_fence isolate { type filter hook forward priority -150 ; policy accept ; }" || return 1
  for ip in "${peers[@]}"; do
    docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$leader_ip" ip daddr "$ip" counter drop || return 1
    docker exec "$node" nft add rule ip pgha_fence isolate ip saddr "$ip" ip daddr "$leader_ip" counter drop || return 1
  done
  cut_done=yes
}

cleanup() {
  if [ -n "$writer_pid" ]; then kill "$writer_pid" 2>/dev/null || true; fi
  if [ -n "$node" ]; then
    if [ "$(docker inspect -f '{{.State.Running}}' "$node" 2>/dev/null || echo false)" != "true" ]; then
      echo "cleanup: starting $node again"
      docker start "$node" >/dev/null || true
    elif [ -n "$cut_done" ]; then
      echo "cleanup: removing the replication cut"
      docker exec "$node" nft delete table ip pgha_fence 2>/dev/null || true
    fi
  fi
  rm -rf "$WDIR"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"
leader=$(current_leader)
node=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$leader" -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
leader_ip=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$leader" -o jsonpath='{.status.podIP}')
pods_json=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pods -l application=pg-ha,cluster-name=pg-ha -o json)
mapfile -t peers < <(jq -r --arg o "$leader" '.items[] | select(.metadata.name != $o) | .status.podIP' <<<"$pods_json")
mapfile -t peer_pods < <(jq -r --arg o "$leader" '.items[] | select(.metadata.name != $o) | .metadata.name' <<<"$pods_json")
sync_before=$(psql_pod "$leader" "show synchronous_standby_names")
echo "leader: $leader on $node; replicas: ${peer_pods[*]}; synchronous_standby_names before: $sync_before"
[ -n "$sync_before" ] || fail "synchronous mode is not active before the test"

psql_pod "$leader" "drop table if exists degraded_probe" >/dev/null 2>&1
psql_pod "$leader" "create table degraded_probe(n int primary key)" >/dev/null

# The writer runs inside the primary's pod: synchronous_commit on (default), no statement timeout.
# A blocked commit therefore shows up as a gap between two "ack" lines.
# shellcheck disable=SC2016
timeout 1500 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader" -c postgres -- bash -c '
  i=0
  while true; do
    i=$((i + 1))
    if psql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c "insert into degraded_probe(n) values ($i)" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
      echo "ack $i $(date +%s%3N)"
    else
      echo "err $i $(date +%s%3N)"
    fi
    sleep 0.5
  done' >"$WDIR/writer.log" 2>/dev/null &
writer_pid=$!
sleep 6
[ "$(acks)" -ge 5 ] || fail "the writer is not acknowledging writes before the cut ($(acks))"
pass "writer acknowledging writes before the cut ($(acks) acks)"

cut || fail "the nft rules could not be installed, so there is no cut to test"
cut_ms=$(now_ms)
echo "cut: $leader is cut off from ${peer_pods[*]} (API access stays up) at $(date +%T)"

degraded_ms=""
t0=$SECONDS
while [ $((SECONDS - t0)) -lt "$MAX_DEGRADE_S" ]; do
  sleep 5
  names=""; ok=""
  if names=$(psql_pod "$leader" "show synchronous_standby_names" 2>/dev/null); then ok=yes; fi
  echo "t+$((SECONDS - t0))s synchronous_standby_names='${names}' acks=$(acks)"
  if [ -n "$ok" ] && [ -z "$names" ]; then degraded_ms=$(now_ms); break; fi
done
[ -n "$degraded_ms" ] || fail "Patroni did not fall back to asynchronous within ${MAX_DEGRADE_S} s; commits stay blocked"
echo "MEASURED: Patroni switched to asynchronous $(((degraded_ms - cut_ms) / 1000)) s after the cut"
pass "Patroni fell back to asynchronous replication on its own"

a0=$(acks)
sleep 15
a1=$(acks)
echo "acknowledged writes in the 15 s after the switch: $((a1 - a0))"
[ $((a1 - a0)) -ge 5 ] || fail "writes did not resume after the switch to asynchronous"
pass "writes resumed after the fall back"
max_gap=$(awk '$1=="ack" { if (prev) { g=$3-prev; if (g>m) m=g } prev=$3 } END { print m+0 }' "$WDIR/writer.log")
echo "MEASURED: longest gap between acknowledged writes (commit stall): ${max_gap} ms"

leader_n=$(psql_pod "$leader" "select count(*) from degraded_probe")
for p in "${peer_pods[@]}"; do
  c=$(psql_pod "$p" "select count(*) from degraded_probe")
  echo "rows on $p: $c, rows on the primary $leader: $leader_n"
  [ "$c" -lt "$leader_n" ] || fail "replica $p has all the rows; the cut did not isolate it"
done
pass "the replicas are behind: writes acknowledged in the degraded state exist only on the primary"

echo "powering off node $node (docker kill) at $(date +%T)"
docker kill "$node" >/dev/null
cut_done=""
sleep 1
kill "$writer_pid" 2>/dev/null || true
writer_pid=""
cp "$WDIR/writer.log" docs/evidence/ADR1-degraded-writer.log
acked_total=$(grep -c '^ack' "$WDIR/writer.log" || true)
echo "acknowledged writes in total: $acked_total"

echo "holding the node down for ${HOLD_S} s and watching whether a replica promotes itself"
t0=$SECONDS
promoted=""
while [ $((SECONDS - t0)) -lt "$HOLD_S" ]; do
  for p in "${peer_pods[@]}"; do
    if [ "$(in_recovery "$p")" = "f" ]; then promoted="$p"; fi
  done
  [ -z "$promoted" ] || break
  sleep 5
done
for p in "${peer_pods[@]}"; do
  c=$(kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$p" -c postgres --since="$((HOLD_S + 20))s" 2>/dev/null | grep -c "not the healthiest node" || true)
  echo "log lines 'not the healthiest node' on $p during the hold: $c"
done
[ -z "$promoted" ] || fail "replica $promoted promoted itself without a primary and without a sync standby; acknowledged writes can be lost"
pass "no replica promoted itself during ${HOLD_S} s (Patroni refuses to fail over to a non-synchronous replica)"

echo "starting node $node again at $(date +%T)"
docker start "$node" >/dev/null
start_ms=$(now_ms)
wp=""
for _ in $(seq 1 90); do
  if wp=$(writable_pod) && psql_pod "$wp" "insert into degraded_probe(n) values (100000) on conflict do nothing" >/dev/null 2>&1; then break; fi
  wp=""
  sleep 3
done
[ -n "$wp" ] || fail "no writable primary came back after the node was restarted"
echo "MEASURED: seconds from restarting the node to a writable primary: $(( ($(now_ms) - start_ms) / 1000 ))"
echo "writable primary after the restart: $wp (the old primary was $leader)"
pass "a writable primary is back without any manual step"

psql_pod "$wp" "select n from degraded_probe" > "$WDIR/present.txt"
awk 'NR==FNR { p[$1]=1; next } $1=="ack" && !($2 in p) { print $2 }' "$WDIR/present.txt" "$WDIR/writer.log" > "$WDIR/lost.txt"
lost=$(wc -l < "$WDIR/lost.txt")
echo "acknowledged writes: $acked_total, rows on the new primary: $(wc -l < "$WDIR/present.txt"), acknowledged writes missing: $lost"
[ "$lost" = 0 ] || fail "$lost acknowledged write(s) are missing after recovery"
pass "no acknowledged write was lost"

wait_healthy 600 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table
want=$(psql_pod "$wp" "select count(*) from degraded_probe")
for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
  ok=""
  for _ in $(seq 1 10); do
    c=$(psql_pod "$p" "select count(*) from degraded_probe" || true)
    if [ "$c" = "$want" ]; then ok=yes; break; fi
    sleep 3
  done
  echo "rows on $p: ${c:-?} (expected $want)"
  [ -n "$ok" ] || fail "$p does not match the primary after recovery"
done
pass "all three nodes hold the same rows"

restored=""
names=""
cur=$(writable_pod)
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  names=$(psql_pod "$cur" "show synchronous_standby_names" || true)
  if [ -n "$names" ]; then restored=yes; break; fi
  sleep 4
done
[ -n "$restored" ] || fail "synchronous mode did not come back after the replicas returned"
pass "synchronous mode came back on its own (synchronous_standby_names=$names)"

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
shellcheck tests/rep-degraded-sync.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"

4. Update the ADR and the limitations (after the run passes)
tee -a docs/decisions/0001-sync-vs-async-replication.md > /dev/null <<'EOF'

## Amendment 2026-11-20: measured degraded behaviour
Measured with tests/rep-degraded-sync.sh (evidence: docs/evidence/ADR1-degraded*.txt).
- With both replicas cut off from the primary (API access kept), commits stalled for about 61 s, close
  to wal_sender_timeout (1min), before Patroni switched to asynchronous (empty
  synchronous_standby_names). Writes then resumed and the replicas fell behind.
- When the primary's node was then powered off, neither replica took over (first run: about
  3.5 minutes until the node was restarted). Both logged that they were not the healthiest node.
  Patroni's documentation describes this case: with no suitable standby, a failed primary is not
  replaced, and the old primary is promoted when it returns. The log lines do not name the reason;
  the match with the documentation is my reading.
- After the node returned, the old primary became leader again and all acknowledged writes were
  present on all three nodes; synchronous mode came back on its own.

Consequences:
- RPO is 0 for acknowledged commits while promotion can only go to the synchronous standby. In the
  degraded state, commits acknowledged since the fall back exist only on the primary, so losing the
  primary's storage in that state (a double failure) would lose them.
- Price: with no synchronous standby, a lost primary is not replaced automatically; recovery waits
  for the old primary to return or for an operator to promote a replica by hand, accepting data loss.
- Alternatives considered, not adopted: synchronous_mode_strict (writes block instead of falling
  back to async) and synchronous_mode off (failover always possible, RPO above 0).
EOF

tee -a docs/known-limitations.md > /dev/null <<'EOF'

## Degraded synchronous mode
- If both replicas are unreachable, commits stall for about wal_sender_timeout (61 s measured) before
  Patroni falls back to asynchronous. If the primary is then lost, no replica is promoted
  automatically (Patroni synchronous mode); recovery waits for the old primary to return, or for a
  manual promotion that accepts data loss. Measured in tests/rep-degraded-sync.sh. This is a
  deliberate trade of availability for RPO 0.
- A silent partition of a replica delays the primary's detection by wal_sender_timeout; a shorter
  value was not tested.
EOF

PASTED
The degraded-mode test passed and is committed (03ab196). It confirms the explanation from the last round with a second, independent run. Nothing is left behind: all 4 nodes are Ready, no nft table remains, and the cluster is back to 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas.

What the passing run measured
Commit stall: the longest gap between acknowledged writes was 63.6 s, and Patroni switched to async 65 s after the cut. That is consistent with wal_sender_timeout (60 s). I haven’t proven the link, since that would need a run with a different timeout.
Refusal to fail over: with the primary’s node powered off for 90 s, neither replica promoted itself. Each logged not the healthiest node 8 times in that window.
Recovery: 56 s after the node restart, pg-ha-0 was writable again as the old primary, with no manual step.
No data lost: all 45 acknowledged writes were present, and all three nodes held the same 46 rows.
Sync mode returned on its own, with pg-ha-2 as the new sync standby.
The ADR amendment I committed quotes the first run’s numbers (61 s, about 3.5 minutes). Add the second run’s numbers so it matches the evidence:

tee -a docs/decisions/0001-sync-vs-async-replication.md > /dev/null <<'EOF'

Second run (tests/rep-degraded-sync.sh, ADR1-degraded.txt), same contract asserted:
- Patroni switched to asynchronous 65 s after the cut; longest commit stall 63.6 s.
- With the primary's node powered off for 90 s, neither replica promoted itself (8 "not the healthiest
  node" log lines on each); 56 s after the node restart a writable primary was back (the old one).
- 45 of 45 acknowledged writes survived; all three nodes held the same 46 rows afterwards.

Failover numbers for the write-up
Scenario	Measured RTO	Evidence
Primary pod crash (FO-1)	6.7 s, restarted in place, no promotion	FO-1.txt
Node loss (FO-2)	33.4 s, replica promoted	FO-2.txt
Storage loss (FO-3)	8.0 s, replica promoted, old pod re-cloned	FO-3.txt
API partition (FO-4)	11.5 s write gap, old primary demoted at 15 s	FO-4.txt
Phase 2, last step: placement and disruption budget (FT-1, FT-2)
FT-1, spread:

It lists each pod with its node and zone, and requires 3 distinct nodes and 3 distinct zones.
It also proves anti-affinity is enforced and not merely configured: it scales the StatefulSet to 4, requires the extra pod to stay Pending because of anti-affinity, then scales back and removes its PVC.
FT-2, disruption budget:

It checks the static settings: PDB minAvailable: 2, resource requests and a memory limit, the three probes, and the termination grace period.
It then drains the node of an async replica, which the PDB should allow. The pod can’t come back while that node is cordoned, so the budget drops to 0.
It then tries to drain the node of the sync standby, which the PDB must block. The leader is never touched, so there’s no failover.
It checks the cluster still serves writes during this, then uncordons both nodes and requires a full recovery.
1. Write the test
tee tests/ft-spread-pdb.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# FT-1: pods are spread across nodes and zones, and anti-affinity is enforced (a 4th pod cannot be scheduled).
# FT-2: PDB, resource requests/limits, probes and a termination grace period are set, and the PDB
#       blocks a second node drain while one replica is already out.
set -Eeuo pipefail
trap 'echo "FAIL: command failed at line $LINENO: $BASH_COMMAND"' ERR
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

drained=""
cleanup() {
  kubectl -n "$NS" scale sts/pg-ha --replicas=3 >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  kubectl -n "$NS" delete pvc data-pg-ha-3 --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  for n in $drained; do kubectl uncordon "$n" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true; done
}
trap cleanup EXIT

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "cluster is not healthy before the test"

echo "--- FT-1: placement ---"
sts=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get sts pg-ha -o json)
jq -e '.spec.template.spec.affinity.podAntiAffinity.requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution | length > 0' <<<"$sts" >/dev/null \
  || fail "no required pod anti-affinity on the StatefulSet"
pass "required pod anti-affinity (one pod per node) is configured"
jq -e '.spec.template.spec.topologySpreadConstraints | map(select(.topologyKey == "topology.kubernetes.io/zone" and .whenUnsatisfiable == "DoNotSchedule")) | length > 0' <<<"$sts" >/dev/null \
  || fail "no zone topology spread constraint with DoNotSchedule"
pass "zone topology spread constraint (DoNotSchedule) is configured"

pods_json=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pods -l application=pg-ha,cluster-name=pg-ha -o json)
echo "pod -> node -> zone:"
zones_list=""
for row in $(jq -r '.items[] | .metadata.name + "=" + .spec.nodeName' <<<"$pods_json" | sort); do
  p=${row%%=*}; nd=${row#*=}
  z=$(kubectl get node "$nd" -o jsonpath='{.metadata.labels.topology\.kubernetes\.io/zone}')
  echo "  $p -> $nd -> $z"
  zones_list="$zones_list $z"
done
nodes_n=$(jq -r '.items[].spec.nodeName' <<<"$pods_json" | sort -u | wc -l)
zones_n=$(tr ' ' '\n' <<<"$zones_list" | sort -u | grep -c . || true)
[ "$nodes_n" = 3 ] || fail "expected the 3 pods on 3 distinct nodes, found $nodes_n"
pass "3 pods on 3 distinct nodes"
[ "$zones_n" = 3 ] || fail "expected 3 distinct zones, found $zones_n"
pass "3 pods in 3 distinct zones"

echo "scaling to 4 replicas to prove anti-affinity is enforced"
kubectl -n "$NS" scale sts/pg-ha --replicas=4 >/dev/null
msg=""; phase=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  phase=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod pg-ha-3 -o jsonpath='{.status.phase}' 2>/dev/null || true)
  msg=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod pg-ha-3 -o jsonpath='{.status.conditions[?(@.type=="PodScheduled")].message}' 2>/dev/null || true)
  if [ "$phase" = Pending ] && [ -n "$msg" ]; then break; fi
  sleep 3
done
echo "scheduler message for the 4th pod: ${msg:-<none>}"
[ "$phase" = Pending ] || fail "the 4th pod is '$phase'; it should stay Pending"
grep -qi "anti-affinity" <<<"$msg" || fail "the 4th pod is Pending, but not because of anti-affinity: $msg"
pass "a 4th pod cannot be scheduled: anti-affinity is enforced"
kubectl -n "$NS" scale sts/pg-ha --replicas=3 >/dev/null
kubectl -n "$NS" delete pvc data-pg-ha-3 --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null

echo "--- FT-2: disruption budget, resources, probes ---"
min=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pdb pg-ha -o jsonpath='{.spec.minAvailable}')
[ "$min" = 2 ] || fail "PDB minAvailable is '$min', expected 2"
pass "PodDisruptionBudget minAvailable = 2"
c=$(jq '.spec.template.spec.containers[0]' <<<"$sts")
jq -e '.resources.requests.cpu and .resources.requests.memory and .resources.limits.memory' <<<"$c" >/dev/null \
  || fail "resource requests or the memory limit are missing"
pass "resource requests and a memory limit are set"
jq -e '.startupProbe and .livenessProbe and .readinessProbe' <<<"$c" >/dev/null || fail "a probe is missing"
pass "startup, liveness and readiness probes are set"
grace=$(jq -r '.spec.template.spec.terminationGracePeriodSeconds' <<<"$sts")
[ "$grace" -ge 30 ] || fail "terminationGracePeriodSeconds is $grace"
pass "terminationGracePeriodSeconds = $grace"

leader=$(current_leader)
async=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Replica") | .Member' | head -1)
syncpod=$(cluster_json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Sync Standby") | .Member' | head -1)
[ -n "$async" ] && [ -n "$syncpod" ] || fail "need one async replica and one sync standby"
node_a=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$async" -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
node_b=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$syncpod" -o jsonpath='{.spec.nodeName}')
echo "leader $leader (untouched); async replica $async on $node_a; sync standby $syncpod on $node_b"
drained="$node_a $node_b"

echo "draining $node_a (one replica goes out)"
timeout 170 kubectl drain "$node_a" --ignore-daemonsets --delete-emptydir-data --timeout=150s
pass "the PDB allowed one replica to be evicted"
allowed=""
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
  allowed=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pdb pg-ha -o jsonpath='{.status.disruptionsAllowed}')
  if [ "$allowed" = 0 ]; then break; fi
  sleep 3
done
echo "PDB disruptionsAllowed with one replica out: $allowed"
[ "$allowed" = 0 ] || fail "PDB still allows $allowed disruption(s) with one replica out"
pass "PDB now allows 0 further disruptions"

rw=$(sql_primary "select pg_is_in_recovery()") || fail "cannot reach the primary while a node is drained"
[ "$rw" = "f" ] || fail "pg-primary is not a primary while a node is drained: $rw"
pass "the cluster still serves writes while a replica is out"

echo "trying to drain $node_b (hosts the sync standby $syncpod) while $async is out"
if out=$(timeout 120 kubectl drain "$node_b" --ignore-daemonsets --delete-emptydir-data --timeout=40s 2>&1); then
  printf '%s\n' "$out" | tail -6
  fail "the drain of $node_b succeeded; the PDB did not protect the cluster"
fi
printf '%s\n' "$out" | tail -6
grep -qi "disruption budget" <<<"$out" || fail "the drain failed, but not because of the disruption budget"
pass "the PDB blocked a second drain (it would have left fewer than 2 pods available)"
phase=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get pod "$syncpod" -o jsonpath='{.status.phase}')
[ "$phase" = Running ] || fail "the sync standby $syncpod is '$phase' after the blocked drain"
pass "the sync standby $syncpod is untouched and still Running"

echo "uncordoning both nodes"
kubectl uncordon "$node_a" "$node_b" >/dev/null
drained=""
wait_healthy 400 || { cluster_table || true; fail "cluster did not return to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"; }
cluster_table
pass "cluster is back to 1 leader + 2 streaming replicas"
kubectl -n "$NS" get pdb pg-ha

n=$(writable_primaries)
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "$n pods accept writes, expected exactly 1"
pass "exactly one pod accepts writes"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/ft-spread-pdb.sh
shellcheck tests/ft-spread-pdb.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"
2. Run it

All 4 nodes should be Ready with no SchedulingDisabled. The StatefulSet should be 3/3, there should be 3 PVCs (data-pg-ha-0 to -2), and the cluster should show 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas.

Plain text is a better format for this log than a PDF, so export the Google Doc as .txt instead. Your doc looks right: the first message sits at the top, and the sidebar and your other chats didn’t come along. Three reasons for text:

The redaction script and gitleaks only work on text. They can’t scan or redact a PDF reliably.
Reviewers can search text. That makes it easier to check your claims against the log.
A PDF made from a doc made from a copy adds a layer of reformatting, and nothing is gained from it.

Step 3: a reusable completeness check
Instead of pasting a long command block every time, this saves the check as a script and commits it. It makes the log process repeatable for a reviewer too. It prints counts only, never chat text:

tee scripts/check-log.sh > /dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Completeness check for a captured chat log. Prints counts only, never chat text.
# Exit code 1 if any section heading of the assistant's replies is missing.
set -euo pipefail
F=${1:?usage: scripts/check-log.sh <file>}
missing=0
wc -l -c "$F"
markers=(
  "What they want, in short"
  "Best practices for this task"
  "Plan before we start"
  "Phase 1, step 1: pin versions"
  "Phase 1, step 1: session log redaction"
  "Phase 2, step 1: kind cluster"
  "Phase 2, step 2: the image"
  "Phase 2, step 3: deploy Patroni"
  "Phase 2, step 4: the first failover test"
  "Phase 2, step 5: node loss"
  "Phase 2, step 6: storage loss"
  "Phase 2, step 7: split-brain"
  "Phase 2, step 8: FO-5"
  "Phase 2, step 9: REP-3 drill"
  "Phase 2, step 10: degraded synchronous mode"
  "Phase 2, last step: placement"
  "Phase 2 summary"
)
for m in "${markers[@]}"; do
  n=$(grep -c -- "$m" "$F" || true)
  printf '%-46s %s\n' "$m" "$n"
  if [ "$n" -lt 1 ]; then missing=$((missing + 1)); fi
done
echo "lines with the user's terminal prompt: $(grep -c 'hmsmiraz@HMSMIRAZ' "$F" || true)"
echo "mentions of the brief title: $(grep -c 'Take-Home Assignment' "$F" || true)"
echo "headings missing: $missing"
[ "$missing" -eq 0 ]
EOF
chmod +x scripts/check-log.sh
shellcheck scripts/check-log.sh && echo "shellcheck OK"
Run it on the new copy:

bash scripts/check-log.sh docs/ai-process/raw/2026-11-20-claude-chat-share-copy.txt
Step 4: commit the script and the .gitignore change
git status --short
git add .gitignore scripts/check-log.sh
git commit -m "chore: ignore Zone.Identifier files; add log completeness check"
git push
git status --short should show only those two files. Nothing from raw/ may appear.
